# Domain 01 — Business Objective

Business Objective

Certification Run:

2026-07-06 — CANONICAL_PROMPT_LOCKED / Version 1.0 Validation Law

## Executive Summary

- Domain analyzed: `01 Business Objective`.
- Mission discovered: define why V7 exists and what production outcome all other domains must serve.
- Ideal Domain Candidate: keep Business Objective as the first independent product-purpose domain.
- Strongest finding: V7's real product is reliable invisible production connectivity, not VPN protocol operation, dashboarding, reports, or raw automation.
- Ownership finding: Product Specification owns Business Objective; OMP, policies, Runtime, Authority, Production Maturity, CPS, reports, and implementation consume it only after translation.
- Implementation finding: Function Graph contains no direct raw Business Objective mutation owner, and that absence is correct.
- Research finding: R1-R4 independently support user-outcome, evidence, authority, verification, rollback/closure, learning, and earned autonomy boundaries.
- Important gaps: no blocking Domain 01 gap; downstream operator/UI and production-autonomy maturity gaps remain outside this domain.
- Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.
- Recommended next action: proceed to owner review / next domain certification without changing Domain 01 architecture.

## Mission Discovery

| Question | Answer |
| --- | --- |
| Fundamental engineering problem | V7 needs a stable product-level source that defines the production outcome: users should remain connected through safe, verified, evidence-driven routing. |
| Importance for autonomous routing | Without a product objective, downstream systems can optimize internal artifacts such as scores, packets, selected moves, reports, timers, or routes instead of user-visible connectivity. |
| Solved by another domain? | No. Product Principles, System Laws, OMP, Policy, Runtime, and Production Maturity consume or constrain the objective; none should own why V7 exists. |
| Why separate domain? | It is the root mission and Product Owner interface. Merging it into execution or maturity would invert ownership. |
| If it disappeared | V7 would still have mechanics, but no canonical business reason to decide what counts as success, failure, unacceptable risk, or useful autonomy. |
| World-class conceptual solution | Large production systems anchor autonomy in user-impact objectives, SLO-style outcomes, explicit ownership, bounded execution, verification, rollback/closure, and learning. |
| Current V7 intent | Product Specification says V7 keeps users online by automatically finding, proving, and learning the safest working route. Business Objectives are the canonical Product Owner interface. |
| Current implementation reality | Implementation begins downstream. Function Graph shows observation/evidence/planner/runtime/verification/reporting owners, but no raw Business Objective code owner. |
| Ideal solution characteristics | Product-level, owner-readable, independent, consumed only through translation, invariant across protocols and scale, and able to reject false success. |

| Mission Consumers | Product Principles, System Laws, Decision Model, Policy, Planner, Authority, Runtime, Verification, Rollback/Closure, Learning, OMP, Production Maturity, CPS, operator surfaces. |

## Three Independent Experts

### V7 Project Expert Output

Inside V7, Business Objective should be the first architectural domain because it defines the product outcome all other domains serve. Product Specification owns this meaning. V7 exists to keep users online by making routing reliable and invisible, while minimizing disruption, risk, and operator work. Business Objectives are Product Owner language and must be translated before they reach policies, OMP, Authority, Runtime, Verification, Rollback/Closure, Learning, Production Maturity, or CPS. The V7 Project Expert found no canonical source that moves Business Objective ownership away from Product Specification.

### World Research Expert Output

Universal engineering principles support an independent Business Objective domain. Large autonomous production systems must optimize user-visible outcomes rather than internal action. They require explicit ownership, real evidence, bounded authority, verification, rollback/closure, learning, and staged maturity. Research rejects blind automation, synthetic-only production certification, timer-as-authority, report-as-capability, and "automate everything" as universal product principles. These principles strengthen V7's product mission rather than requiring a new architecture.

### Implementation Expert Output

Implementation reality does not contain a direct raw Business Objective mutation owner. The Function Graph shows downstream implementation surfaces for runtime support, service evidence, autoswitch/planner, governed execution, verification, reporting, read-only/admin views, tests, and systemd entrypoints. This is correct for Domain 01: product objective is a canonical source, not an executable function. No implementation evidence requires merging Business Objective into Runtime, OMP, Authority, or reports.

## Ideal Domain Discovery

The strongest evidence-supported mission is not "run VPNs" or "move users automatically." It is: preserve real user connectivity as a managed production outcome. The domain must describe what V7 is for, who benefits, what counts as success or failure, and what V7 is not allowed to pretend. It must remain independent because every downstream domain needs a stable product source, while none of them should define the product's reason to exist.

## Ideal Domain Candidate

| Field | Candidate |
| --- | --- |
| Domain name | Business Objective |
| Position | Domain 01 |
| Mission | Define V7's product-purpose: reliable invisible production connectivity for real users. |
| Ownership | Product Specification / Product Owner semantics. |
| Inputs | Product mission, Business Objectives, risk appetite, SLA/service/user fit, ideal user experience, product success/failure. |
| Outputs | Product objective, user value, success criteria, failure criteria, non-goals, constraints for downstream translation. |
| Required boundaries | No runtime mutation, no planner logic, no authority grant, no OMP ownership, no direct code owner, no certification shortcut. |
| Success model | Users stay online or recover safely; important services remain reachable; moves are verified, reversible/closed, minimally disruptive, and autonomy grows only through production evidence. |
| Failure model | Users remain on bad channels despite certified safe recovery path; V7 reports success without service restoration; ordinary incidents require repeated manual rescue; automation grows without evidence. |

## Current Domain Discovery

The current Domain 01 in Knowledge Consolidation already states that V7 exists to keep users online despite changing/degraded channels, services, routes, countries, providers, and operating conditions. It defines V7 as reliable invisible routing and a governed self-improving production routing platform. It includes why V7 exists, primary product, primary user value, success/failure criteria, non-goals, and supporting evidence. Architecture Review and Architecture Tree Audit classify the domain as independent, non-duplicative, and correctly positioned at `01`.

## Architectural Equivalence

`FULLY_EQUIVALENT`

The Ideal Domain Candidate and current Domain 01 are architecturally equivalent. Minor differences are presentation-level: the current domain could expose producer/consumer mapping more explicitly, but the information already exists in Supporting Evidence and Architecture Review. No architecture change is required.

## Domain Existence Review

| Option | Verdict | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Keep as separate Domain 01 | `ACCEPTED` | Product mission must precede laws, principles, policy, execution, and maturity. |
| Merge into Product Principles | `REJECTED` | Principles govern product behavior; they do not replace why the product exists. |
| Merge into OMP | `REJECTED` | OMP executes/continues the program; it must not own product purpose. |
| Merge into System Laws | `REJECTED` | Laws are universal constraints; Business Objective is product-specific mission. |
| Remove domain | `REJECTED` | Would remove the stable product source for downstream domains. |
| Split domain | `REJECTED` | Current responsibilities are cohesive: mission, value, success/failure, non-goals. |

## Knowledge Synthesis

The three realities converge. V7 project knowledge says Product Specification owns product mission and Business Objectives. Research says autonomous production systems must anchor automation in user-visible outcomes and earned capability, not internal action. Implementation reality says raw Business Objective is not executable code and should not mutate production directly. Therefore the strongest architecture is to keep Business Objective as an independent product-purpose domain and treat downstream UI/runtime/maturity/certification gaps as consumers, not as defects of the domain.

| No direct Business Objective code owner is required. | `HIGH` | Function Graph Appendix/JSON, code/domain-term searches, runtime boundary rules. |
| Production autonomy remains earned by evidence, not declared by docs or reports. | `HIGH` | AOS, Production Maturity, R1-R4, CPS. |
| UI/operator Business Objective exposure is downstream and non-blocking for this domain. | `MEDIUM/HIGH` | SYSTEM_MAP and integration audits. |

## Architectural Decision Package

| Field | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Current Domain | Domain 01 — Business Objective. |
| Mission | Define why V7 exists and what production outcome the system must serve. |
| Mission Justification | Downstream autonomy needs a stable product source; otherwise implementation mechanics can masquerade as product success. |
| Mission Alternatives | Merge into Product Principles, OMP, System Laws, code, or remove. |
| Mission Risks | If too vague, becomes slogan-only; if too technical, duplicates downstream domains. Current text avoids both. |
| Ideal Domain Candidate | Independent product-purpose domain with Product Specification ownership and downstream translation. |
| Differences | No architectural difference; current domain could expose producer/consumer mapping more explicitly later. |
| Evidence | Product Specification, Canonical Reference, Master Handoff, OMP, R1-R4, Function Graph. |
| Alternatives considered | A1-A5 above. |
| Reasons alternatives rejected | All alternatives create ownership inversion, duplicate canonical owners, or erase mission-first architecture. |
| Trade-offs | Keeping domain separate requires disciplined cross-domain consumption; this is preferable to mixing product purpose with execution. |
| Architectural risks | Downstream surfaces may under-display business language; not a Domain 01 blocker. |
| Recommendation | Keep Domain 01 unchanged and certify it. |

## Internal Conflict Investigation

Potential conflict investigated: "If implementation has no Business Objective code owner, is Domain 01 incomplete?"

Resolution: No. Domain 01 is the product/canonical source. Runtime must not consume raw Product Owner language, and production mutation requires translated policy, authority, runtime eligibility, verification, and rollback/closure. Absence of a code owner for raw Business Objectives is correct boundary evidence.

# Domain 02 — System Laws

Certification run: `2026-07-06_102803`

Prompt status: `CANONICAL_PROMPT_LOCKED`

Prompt version: `1.0`

Target domain: `02 System Laws`

Append-only output: `docs/reports/research/V7_PHASE1_DOMAIN_CERTIFICATION.md`

## Executive Summary

Domain 02 was analyzed as the universal law layer of V7.
The discovered mission is to define the non-negotiable constraints that every V7 subsystem must obey before any domain-specific design, owner, Runtime path, or certification stage can be trusted.
The Ideal Domain Candidate is a constitutional system-law domain: it names cross-system laws, explains why each law exists, states what breaks when violated, and keeps implementation-specific enforcement in downstream domains.
The current Domain 02 is functionally equivalent to that ideal candidate.
The certification confirmed that System Laws must remain independent from Policy, Decision Model, Runtime, OMP, and Production Maturity.
No blocking architecture gap was found.
The main knowledge delta is boundary clarification: System Laws are not an executable owner and should not have a single implementation module; they are enforced by distributed owners and verified through downstream evidence.
Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.
Recommended next action: proceed to Domain 03 Product Principles.

## Mission Discovery

### Fundamental Engineering Problem

System Laws exist because V7 is a production autonomous routing platform where many owners can observe, decide, authorize, execute, verify, rollback, learn, document, and evolve the system. Without a universal law layer, each owner could optimize locally and still violate the global safety model. The problem solved by Domain 02 is not routing, planning, runtime mutation, or policy expression. The problem is preserving a stable constitutional boundary that tells every part of V7 what must never be violated.

### Why The Problem Matters

The V7 project repeatedly discovered that automation defects came from confusing related but different states: observation with authority, planner decisions with execution, report evidence with production truth, synthetic proof with reality, one user validation with broad production scale, and STOP/rollback with terminal failure instead of useful evidence. A system-law domain prevents those confusions from becoming architectural behavior.

### Could Another Domain Solve This?

No. Policy can encode allowed behavior, but policy is downstream from law. Decision Model can structure choices, but it cannot define the universal constraints that govern every subsystem. Runtime can enforce execution safety, but it must not become the owner of architectural truth. OMP can drive work, but it consumes laws rather than defining them. Production Maturity can recognize capability, but capability is constrained by laws before it is promoted.

### What If The Domain Disappeared?

If System Laws disappeared, V7 would still contain scattered rules in AOS, Master Handoff, Decision Model, Runtime Model, Production Maturity, ADRs, and engineering reports, but there would be no single owner-readable law layer. That would increase duplicate rules, owner drift, report-driven authority, hidden synthetic certification, and local fixes that pass tests while violating global autonomy constraints.

### World-Class Conceptual Solution

World-class production autonomous systems separate universal engineering laws from implementation mechanisms. The stable laws are: reality before authority, detection before diagnosis, decision before execution but not equal to execution, bounded authority before mutation, verification after mutation, rollback or closure after risk, explicit unknowns, evidence freshness, learning from terminal outcomes, single durable owners for truth, and human ownership of policy exceptions and architecture boundaries.

### V7 Current Intent

V7 intends System Laws to be the cross-domain rule layer. AOS names Fundamental Autonomy Laws. Master Handoff names immutable rules and Reality First. Canonical Reference names the universal law hierarchy. R4 extracts universal laws from external research. The Knowledge Consolidation materializes these laws as Domain 02.

### Implementation Reality

The implementation does not contain a single `System Laws` runtime module, and that absence is correct. Function Graph evidence shows 3,439 indexed nodes, 820 mutation-capable nodes, 1,202 read-only/advisory nodes, 922 authority-marked nodes, 13 systemd units, and 24,821 static edges. The laws are therefore enforced as distributed constraints across mutation boundaries, authority paths, runtime support, verification, rollback/closure, safety reviews, certification tools, and reporting paths.

### Ideal Solution Characteristics

An ideal System Laws domain should be independent, concise, owner-readable, implementation-neutral, evidence-backed, non-duplicative, and strong enough to constrain every downstream domain. It should explain why each law exists, what breaks if violated, and which canonical sources support it. It should not own code paths, production mutation, owner invocation, certification state, or domain-specific policy.

## Ideal Domain Discovery

### V7 Project Expert Output

The V7 Project Expert concludes that System Laws should be the project-wide constitutional layer. It must define the laws every domain follows: Reality First, evidence before capability, authority before mutation, decision/execution separation, object continuity, safety and blast-radius discipline, verification and rollback/closure, unknown/freshness handling, learning, owner reuse, single durable truth ownership, evidence/report boundaries, suspendable automation, manual work debt, human policy boundaries, and incremental evolution.

This expert did not use R1-R4 or Function Graph for its primary answer. It used V7 canonical sources only.

### World Research Expert Output

The World Research Expert concludes that autonomous production systems require universal engineering laws before implementation mechanisms. Independent research supports: real-world evidence before action, diagnosis before correction, explicit authority and blast-radius bounds, identity continuity, verification, rollback/closure, explicit unknown states, freshness proportional to action risk, negative evidence as learning, durable truth ownership, suspendable automation, and human ownership of policy and exception boundaries.

This expert did not use V7 docs or Function Graph for its primary answer. It used R1-R4 only.

### Implementation Expert Output

The Implementation Expert concludes that V7 implements law-relevant behavior as distributed owner surfaces rather than a single `System Laws` program. The implementation has many mutation-capable nodes, read-only/advisory nodes, authority-marked nodes, CLI entrypoints, systemd units, closure states, and test references. Function Graph confirms the importance of distinguishing read-only evidence from mutation, authority from action, runtime support from owner truth, and closed implementation paths from orphan/stub/dormant nodes.

This expert did not evaluate architecture or research. It described implementation reality only.

## Ideal Domain Candidate

### Candidate

`System Laws` should remain an independent architectural domain whose mission is to define the universal, non-negotiable laws that govern all V7 domains. The domain should:

- define each fundamental law in owner-readable language;
- explain why each law exists;
- state what breaks if it is violated;
- describe relationships between laws;
- identify non-negotiable laws;
- cite supporting evidence;
- avoid implementation, API, function, owner-invocation, Runtime, Planner, Authority, OMP, or Production Maturity detail;
- serve as the stable constraint layer consumed by every downstream domain.

### Mission Justification

The mission deserves its own domain because no downstream owner can safely define the rules that constrain all downstream owners. Runtime cannot be the universal law owner because Runtime is itself governed by laws. Policy cannot be the universal law owner because policy is one mechanism for expressing allowed behavior. OMP cannot be the universal law owner because OMP executes and prioritizes work using the laws. Production Maturity cannot be the universal law owner because maturity recognizes earned capability under the laws.

### Mission Alternatives

| Alternative | Summary | Strengths | Weaknesses | Score | Result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| A | Independent System Laws domain | Clear constitutional owner; avoids downstream duplication; matches V7/R4/research/implementation boundary | Requires careful discipline not to include implementation details | 96 | Accepted |
| B | Merge into Policy | Places constraints near admission logic | Policy would become too broad and would own rules that also constrain non-policy domains | 42 | Rejected |
| C | Merge into Runtime | Places safety laws near execution | Runtime would become architecture owner and violate thin runtime/no duplicate owner discipline | 28 | Rejected |
| D | Merge into OMP | Puts laws near program execution | OMP would mix work navigation with architectural law | 35 | Rejected |
| E | Keep laws only in R4 research | Avoids duplicate documentation | Research would become canonical authority, contradicting reports/evidence-not-authority discipline | 25 | Rejected |

## Current Domain Discovery

The current Domain 02 defines System Laws as fundamental rules that govern the whole system and answer what V7 must never violate. It lists 21 laws:

1. Reality First.
2. Evidence Before Capability.
3. Authority Before Production Mutation.
4. Decision Is Not Execution.
5. Object Continuity.
6. Detection Is Not Diagnosis.
7. Safety Before Confidence.
8. Blast Radius Before Scale.
9. Verification Completes Mutation.
10. Rollback Or Closure Is Mandatory.
11. Unknown Is Not Pass And Not Fail.
12. Freshness Matches Action Risk.
13. Learn From Terminal Outcomes.
14. Negative Evidence Is Evidence.
15. Existing Owner Before New Owner.
16. Durable Truth Has One Canonical Owner.
17. Reports Preserve Evidence, Not Authority.
18. Automation Must Be Suspendable.
19. Repeated Manual Work Is Debt Until Classified.
20. Humans Own Policy, Exceptions, And Architecture Boundaries.
21. Evolution Is Incremental And Reversible Where Possible.

The domain also explains relationships between laws, identifies non-negotiable laws, and provides supporting evidence for every law.

This is not a gap.

## Trusted Knowledge Produced

1. System Laws are the constitutional constraint layer of V7, not a Runtime, Policy, OMP, or Production Maturity subchapter.
2. The current 21-law consolidation is supported by V7 canonical documents, R1-R4 research, ADRs, and implementation evidence.
3. R4's larger law catalog should not be copied one-to-one into Domain 02; Domain 02 owns the universal owner-readable consolidation, while detailed specializations belong to downstream domains.
4. Absence of a single executable `System Laws` module is correct. Function Graph shows distributed law relevance across mutation, authority, read-only, runtime, verification, rollback, reporting, and certification paths.
5. System Laws must constrain every future domain certification and implementation mission but must not directly execute production actions.

## V7 Project Expert Output

The V7 project expects System Laws to be the universal constraints under which all autonomous execution, certification, owner extension, production mutation, and documentation synchronization operate. The project evidence is consistent:

- AOS defines Fundamental Autonomy Laws.
- Master Handoff defines Reality First, Capability Earned, immutable no-duplicate/no-bypass rules, and owner resolution.
- Canonical Reference records the universal law hierarchy and equivalent canonical concepts.
- Decision Model and Runtime Model consume and specialize laws rather than owning them.
- Production Maturity recognizes earned capability under laws.
- ADRs preserve key law-bearing decisions such as authority class, no synthetic evidence, runtime thinness, and research standards.

Conclusion: inside V7, Domain 02 should be a stable law layer, not a workflow, owner, execution model, or report collection.

## World Research Expert Output

R1-R4 independently support the same law model. The external principles are implementation-neutral:

- production reality precedes authority;
- detection alone is not diagnosis;
- decision is not execution;
- production mutation requires bounded authority;
- mutation is incomplete without verification;
- risk must close through rollback or closure;
- unknown and stale evidence must be explicit;
- blast radius must precede scale;
- terminal outcomes must feed learning;
- manual work and repeated workflows become classified debt;
- automation must be suspendable;
- humans retain policy, exception, and architecture-boundary ownership.

Conclusion: a world-class autonomous production system needs a universal law layer before domain-specific implementation.

Function Graph shows implementation reality:

- `nodes`: 3,439.

Representative implementation surfaces include runtime support helpers, autoswitch safety review, control-plane governance checks, autonomy trust evidence inventory, client-speed API state mutation, service-matrix/runtime helpers, restore/verification/certification tools, and systemd entrypoints.

Conclusion: implementation reality supports law distribution. It does not require and should not introduce a single executable `System Laws` owner.

## Knowledge Synthesis

All three realities converge. V7 intent says System Laws must constrain all domains. Research says autonomous production systems require universal laws before execution mechanisms. Implementation says V7 has distributed mutation/authority/read-only/runtime surfaces where these laws matter, and no direct law executable owner is expected.

The strongest justified architectural understanding is: Domain 02 is the constitutional layer of V7. It should define universal laws in clear owner-readable language and leave enforcement details to downstream domains and existing owners.

## Internal Conflict Detection

| Potential conflict | Realities involved | Conflict? | Resolution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Current Domain 02 has 21 laws while R4 has more granular laws. | Current domain vs research | `NO_BLOCKING_CONFLICT` | Current domain consolidates R4 into universal owner-readable laws; downstream domains specialize. |
| Implementation lacks a `System Laws` executable owner. | Current domain vs implementation | `NO_CONFLICT` | Domain is architectural law, not runtime execution. |
| Policy and Runtime contain law-like statements. | V7 docs vs domain boundary | `NO_CONFLICT` | They consume/specialize laws, not own the universal law layer. |

## Internal Conflict Investigation

### Conflict 1: Should R4 Laws Be Copied One-To-One?

Hypothesis A: Domain 02 is incomplete because it does not copy every R4 law one-to-one.

Evidence: R4 contains more granular laws such as health matrix, retry budget, hysteresis, overload protection, synthetic evidence, backtesting, and paved roads.

Counter-evidence: Domain 02 includes broader laws that cover these: Evidence Before Capability, Object Continuity, Safety Before Confidence, Blast Radius Before Scale, Freshness, Unknown handling, Learning, Negative Evidence, Existing Owner, Automation Suspendability, Manual Work Debt, and Incremental Evolution. Later domains explicitly reference law specializations.

Resolution: R4 one-to-one copy is unnecessary and would reduce owner readability. `RESOLVED`.

### Conflict 2: Does Lack Of Code Owner Mean Implementation Gap?

Hypothesis A: If System Laws are important, implementation should contain a System Laws module.

Evidence: Function Graph has no single law owner in the inspected implementation evidence.

Counter-evidence: AOS, Master Handoff, Runtime Model, and Function Graph all support distributed ownership. Function Graph shows laws affect many mutation, authority, runtime, verification, reporting, and read-only boundaries.

Resolution: No implementation gap. `RESOLVED`.

## Hypothesis Evaluation

| Hypothesis | Evidence | Probability | Result |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| H1: Current Domain 02 is architecturally correct | V7 docs, R1-R4, current domain, Function Graph | 0.91 | Accepted |
| H2: Domain 02 should merge into Policy | Some law-like constraints appear in policy evidence taxonomy | 0.03 | Rejected |
| H3: Domain 02 should merge into Runtime | Runtime enforces several laws | 0.02 | Rejected |
| H4: Domain 02 should be replaced by R4 | R4 is richer | 0.02 | Rejected |
| H5: Implementation gap exists because laws lack one code owner | No law module found | 0.02 | Rejected |

## Conflict Resolution Result

`ALL_CONFLICTS_RESOLVED`

No unresolved conflict prevents Gap Discovery or Certification.

| R4 detailed laws are more granular than Domain 02. | R4 vs current list. | Not a gap; intentional consolidation. | `NO` |

## World Research Principles

1. Autonomous production safety begins from real state, not belief, synthetic proof, or timer cadence.
2. Observation and detection are not enough; diagnosis and authority are distinct steps.
3. Decisions must remain separate from execution, and execution must preserve identity.
4. Production mutation requires bounded authority, blast-radius control, and safety gates.
5. Verification and rollback/closure are part of the same production action, not optional follow-ups.
6. Unknown, stale, failed, and negative evidence must remain explicit and useful.
7. Capability is earned through real outcomes and maturity recognition, not declared by documents or constants.
8. Automation should reduce repeated manual work but remain suspendable and owner-bounded.
9. Humans retain policy, exception, and architecture-boundary ownership.
10. Evolution should be incremental, reversible where possible, and learned from terminal outcomes.

## Implementation Evidence

Implementation evidence supports the System Laws mission through distributed surfaces:

- mutation boundaries: 820 mutation-capable nodes;
- read-only/advisory boundaries: 1,202 nodes;
- authority relevance: 922 queried nodes marked with authority relevance;
- closure status: explicit closure categories distinguish closed logic, mutation, read-only, orphan, stub/dormant, and test-only paths;
- systemd surface: 13 units;
- graph scale: 3,439 nodes and 24,821 static edges.

Representative entries confirm the law boundaries:

- `tools/v7-autonomy-trust-evidence-inventory:main` is authority-marked and reads audit, closure, execution, registry, and evidence families.
- `tools/v7-control-plane-governance-check` contains read-only and mutation-adjacent authority checks for rehearsals, apply/restore, fresh retry, monitoring, restore settle, and canary approval.
- `tools/v7-autoswitch-safety-review:main` is a read-only preflight helper and explicitly does not execute autoswitch or write reconnect observation state.
- runtime-support helpers include read/write JSON, subprocess runtime helpers, service matrix probes, and profile/state writers, showing why mutation/read-only and authority boundaries matter.

## Objective Improvements

No immediate architecture, prompt, or implementation improvement is required by this certification.

One future evidence-tooling improvement is supported but not yet actionable under the Validation Law: add semantic law tags to Function Graph only if at least three independent domain certifications show the same evidence-query friction and the weakness materially reduces certification quality.

## Rejected Improvements

| Proposed improvement | Reason rejected |
| --- | --- |
| Add a `System Laws Runtime` owner. | Violates existing owner discipline; laws are architectural constraints, not a runtime service. |
| Copy all R4 laws into Domain 02 one-to-one. | Would reduce readability and duplicate downstream domain responsibilities. |
| Merge System Laws into Policy. | Policy consumes laws and cannot own universal constraints. |
| Merge System Laws into Runtime. | Runtime is governed by laws and must remain an execution/enforcement domain. |
| Update canonical docs during certification. | Prompt requires certification report only; no source document changes. |

## Certification Verdict

`CERTIFIED`

System Laws is certified as the correct independent Domain 02 for the V7 Ideal Autonomous System Model. The current domain is architecturally sound, functionally equivalent to the discovered ideal, and ready for owner review. No implementation patch, architecture redesign, prompt change, or canonical document synchronization is required.

# Domain 03 — Product Principles

Product Principles

## Executive Summary

Domain analyzed: `03 Product Principles`.
Mission discovered: translate Business Objective and System Laws into durable product behavior rules for every downstream V7 domain.
Ideal Domain Candidate: independent product-principles domain that defines user connectivity, safety, explainability, reversibility, evidence, authority, no-duplication, and operator-work reduction as product constraints.
What changed: architecture unchanged; trusted knowledge updated by certifying that product principles are not implementation rules and not policy/runtime authority.
Important gaps: none blocking.
Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.
Recommended next action: proceed to Domain 04 Reality Model.

## Mission Discovery

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Fundamental engineering problem | V7 needs a product-behavior layer that turns the reason V7 exists into stable rules for how the product must behave before technical domains choose mechanisms. |
| Mission justification | Without Product Principles, V7 can drift into protocol, VPN-panel, dashboard, planner, or runtime-centered thinking instead of preserving user connectivity, safety, verification, learning, and reduced operator work. |
| Mission success criteria | Downstream decisions can be explained through user connectivity, safety before movement, evidence, explainability, reversibility, background knowledge/thin runtime, no duplicated systems, and minimal operator work. |
| Mission failure criteria | V7 explains itself through technical artifacts instead of user outcome; automation is justified by convenience instead of evidence; new subsystems appear without proven product necessity. |
| Mission boundaries | Owns product behavior principles. Does not own runtime execution, policy admission, planning, authority, verification, rollback, concrete observation, or implementation code. |
| Mission inputs | Business Objective, System Laws, Product Specification, Canonical Reference, AOS, Master Handoff, research R1-R4, Function Graph implementation reality. |
| Mission outputs | Product-level constraints consumed by Reality Model and all downstream architecture domains. |
| Mission producers | Product Specification, Business Objective, System Laws, Canonical Reference, Master Handoff, research, production forensics. |
| Mission consumers | Reality Model, Observation, Health Evidence, Decision Model, Policy, Planner, Authority, Runtime, Verification, Rollback / Closure, Learning, OMP, Production Maturity, Engineering Automation. |
| Mission alternatives | Merge into Business Objective; merge into System Laws; merge into Policy; remove and rely only on Product Specification. |
| Separate-domain justification | Business Objective says why V7 exists; System Laws say what must never be violated; Product Principles say what the product must feel and behave like for users/operators. That is a distinct responsibility. |
| Mission-structure conclusion | Separate domain justified. |

## Trusted Knowledge Produced

Product Principles are the correct independent domain for translating V7's business objective and system laws into durable product behavior. They keep V7 centered on user connectivity, safety before movement, evidence before trust, minimal operator work, explanation, reversibility, learning from reality, event-driven operation, reuse before rewrite, thin runtime, and no duplicated systems. The domain must remain non-executable and must not replace Policy, Authority, Runtime, Verification, Rollback, or OMP.

## V7 Project Expert Output

Inside V7, Product Principles should be the product-behavior layer. Product Specification and Canonical Reference explicitly define V7 as a production connectivity product, not a VPN panel, dashboard, manual router, static load balancer, or planner playground. Product Principles preserve user connectivity, safety before movement, verification before trust, learning from reality, explainability, reversibility, no duplicated systems, and minimal operator work. They must constrain downstream domains but not become policy admission, runtime execution, or authority.

## World Research Expert Output

World research supports a product-principles layer because mature production systems do not define automation as raw traffic movement. They separate product outcomes from mechanisms, use bounded automation, verify outcomes, preserve rollback/closure, learn from real incidents, reduce operator toil through owner-mapped workflows, and reject synthetic-only confidence or blind timer authority. These are product behavior principles, not implementation copies.

## Implementation Expert Output

Function Graph shows product principles are not a single executable module. Domain-relevant search across product/operator/admin/intelligence/advisory surfaces matched 2,097 nodes, including 344 mutation-capable nodes, 987 read-only nodes, and 551 authority-marked nodes. This shows broad consumption of product constraints and supports the non-executable boundary of Product Principles.

## Knowledge Synthesis

All three realities converge: V7 needs Product Principles as a stable product-behavior layer; research supports such constraints; implementation consumes them indirectly. The current Domain 03 correctly expresses that layer and does not overclaim authority or mutation.

# Domain 04 — Reality Model

Reality Model

## Executive Summary

Domain analyzed: `04 Reality Model`.
Mission discovered: define what V7 may treat as current production reality before observation, evidence, decisions, authority, or execution consume it.
Ideal Domain Candidate: independent reality-semantics domain that separates current production facts, stale knowledge, synthetic examples, reports, assumptions, unknowns, and terminal outcomes.
What changed: architecture unchanged; trusted knowledge updated by certifying that Reality Model is context/truth semantics, not signal collection or action authority.
Important gaps: none blocking.
Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.
Recommended next action: proceed to Domain 05 Observation.

## Mission Discovery

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Fundamental engineering problem | V7 needs a canonical distinction between real current production state and non-reality objects such as stale reports, dry-runs, synthetic examples, isolated metrics, guesses, planner assumptions, or documentation. |
| Mission justification | A production autonomous routing platform can harm users if it treats an old report, partial metric, or synthetic proof as current reality. |
| Mission success criteria | V7 always knows whether an input is current production fact, stale knowledge, synthetic evidence, historical artifact, unknown state, or verified terminal outcome. |
| Mission failure criteria | Stale reports, dry-runs, planner-only assumptions, or isolated metrics become accepted as reality; unknown becomes pass/fail; synthetic evidence certifies production capability. |
| Mission boundaries | Owns reality semantics and classification. Does not collect signals, diagnose causes, select action, admit authority, mutate production, verify action, or update maturity by itself. |
| Mission inputs | Product Principles, System Laws, production evidence, evidence freshness, terminal outcomes, current state sources, historical reports. |
| Mission outputs | Reality context consumed by Observation, Health Evidence, Wake, Incident, Diagnosis, Decision Model, Planner, Authority, Runtime, Verification, OMP, CPS, and Production Maturity. |
| Mission producers | Production systems, observation owners, verification/rollback/learning owners, Current Program State, engineering reports as evidence. |
| Mission consumers | Observation, Health Evidence, Wake, Incident, Diagnosis, Decision Model, Policy, Planner, Authority, Runtime, Verification, Rollback / Closure, Learning, OMP, Production Maturity. |
| Mission alternatives | Merge into Observation; merge into Health Evidence; merge into Current Program State; merge into System Laws. |
| Separate-domain justification | Reality Model defines what counts as reality. Observation collects facts; Health Evidence structures health; CPS stores volatile state. None should own the universal reality semantics alone. |
| Mission-structure conclusion | Separate domain justified. |

| Mission Consumers | All domains that reason about production state. |

## Trusted Knowledge Produced

Reality Model is the correct independent domain for distinguishing current production reality from stale reports, dry-runs, synthetic examples, planner-only assumptions, historical evidence, isolated metrics, unknown states, and terminal outcomes. It provides context, not authority. Observation can collect and update facts, but it must not collapse one signal into the whole reality. CPS may expose volatile current state, but it does not replace Reality Model semantics.

## V7 Project Expert Output

Inside V7, Reality Model should define what counts as current production reality. Master Handoff and AOS make Reality First foundational: real production evidence overrides synthetic examples, stale reports, report-only claims, planner-only assumptions, and guesses. CPS and Production Maturity consume evidence but do not grant capability or replace producers.

## World Research Expert Output

World research supports separating observed state, health classification, authority, bounded execution, verification, rollback, learning, and maturity. R4 adds the strongest law: real evidence precedes authority and synthetic evidence cannot certify production reality. Therefore this domain must preserve evidence class and reality status before downstream domains act.

## Implementation Expert Output

Function Graph shows many state, registry, snapshot, readiness, quality, runtime-contract, observation, and evidence surfaces: 1,002 matched nodes, including 232 mutation, 368 read-only, and 198 authority-marked nodes. This supports distributed reality inputs and consumers, not a single action-owning Reality Model executor.

## Knowledge Synthesis

The strongest understanding is that Reality Model is an independent semantic context domain. It defines what V7 can treat as real now, what is stale or synthetic, what is unknown, and what has been verified. It is consumed before Observation/Health/Wake/Incident and later decision/execution domains, but it does not authorize action.

# Domain 05 — Observation

Observation

## Executive Summary

Domain analyzed: `05 Observation`.
Mission discovered: make real production facts visible with identity, freshness, and scope, without turning detection into diagnosis, authority, or execution.
Ideal Domain Candidate: independent observation domain that records observable production facts about users, channels, services, routes, failures, and environment and passes them to Health Evidence.
What changed: architecture unchanged; trusted knowledge updated by certifying Observation as producer of facts, not a decision or authority domain.
Important gaps: none blocking.
Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.
Recommended next action: proceed to Domain 06 Health Evidence.

## Mission Discovery

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Fundamental engineering problem | V7 needs a domain that notices production facts before health classification, wake, incident, diagnosis, planning, authority, or execution can happen. |
| Mission justification | Without Observation, V7 waits for humans or acts blind; with overloaded Observation, one signal could wrongly become diagnosis or permission. |
| Mission success criteria | Important facts about users, channels, services, route quality, failure signals, service reachability, scope, and freshness become visible and traceable. |
| Mission failure criteria | Real failures remain invisible; signals lose user/channel/service binding; freshness is unknown; a symptom is treated as diagnosis or action authority. |
| Mission boundaries | Owns fact observation and handoff. Does not own diagnosis, health matrix interpretation, wake admission, incident scope, planning, authority, runtime execution, verification, rollback, or learning. |
| Mission inputs | Reality Model context, production signals, registries/state, service checks, quality observations, route/service/user evidence. |
| Mission outputs | Observed facts with identity, freshness, and affected scope for Health Evidence and downstream consumers. |
| Mission producers | Production systems, probes, state readers, registries, service-matrix/quality surfaces, observability tools. |
| Mission consumers | Health Evidence, Wake, Incident, Diagnosis, Planner, Verification, Engineering Reports, OMP, Production Maturity. |
| Mission alternatives | Merge into Reality Model; merge into Health Evidence; merge into Wake; merge into Runtime. |
| Separate-domain justification | Observation produces facts; Reality Model defines what reality means; Health Evidence structures facts; Wake decides if facts are a legal trigger. These must remain separate. |
| Mission-structure conclusion | Separate domain justified. |

| Mission Boundaries | Fact observation only; no diagnosis, planning, authority, mutation, verification, or closure. |
| Mission Inputs | Current reality context, production state, service/quality/registry signals. |
| Mission Outputs | Observed fact records and handoff to Health Evidence. |
| Mission Producers | Probes, state readers, registries, observability tools. |
| Mission Consumers | Health Evidence and all downstream reasoning/action domains. |

## Trusted Knowledge Produced

Observation is the correct independent domain for making production facts visible. It produces scoped, fresh, reality-linked facts, but does not diagnose root cause, admit wake, define incident scope, plan movement, authorize action, execute runtime behavior, verify outcomes, or close rollback. Its main product value is preventing blind operation while avoiding detection-as-authority mistakes.

## V7 Project Expert Output

Inside V7, Observation should make current production facts visible: users, channels, services, routes, unhealthy/failing/stale/recovering paths, scope, and freshness. It must support user connectivity by feeding downstream evidence while remaining separate from diagnosis, wake, incident, authority, and runtime.

## World Research Expert Output

Research requires separating observation/detection from authority, diagnosis, and execution. Mature systems use telemetry and reason-code evidence as inputs to health and incident processes, not as direct permission to change production.

## Implementation Expert Output

Function Graph shows observation-adjacent implementation surfaces across state, registry, service, quality, matrix, and observability families. The query matched 815 nodes, including read-only and evidence-writing paths. This supports Observation as implemented/connected, not doc-only, while preserving downstream authority boundaries.

## Knowledge Synthesis

Observation is a fact-producing domain. It is necessary, connected to implementation, aligned with research, and architecturally separate from Reality Model and Health Evidence. The current domain expresses this correctly.

# Domain 06 — Health Evidence

Health Evidence

## Executive Summary

Domain analyzed: `06 Health Evidence`.
Mission discovered: turn observed facts into structured, inspectable health evidence without collapsing health into one opaque boolean or score.
Ideal Domain Candidate: independent evidence domain that preserves source, target, service, freshness, load, safety, reason, producer, owner, probe, raw result, and consumer path.
What changed: architecture unchanged; trusted knowledge updated by certifying Health Evidence as diagnostics/evidence, not Wake, Incident, Planner, Authority, Runtime, or Verification outcome.
Important gaps: none blocking.
Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.
Recommended next action: proceed to Domain 07 Intelligence.

## Mission Discovery

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Fundamental engineering problem | V7 needs structured health proof so downstream domains can know what is healthy, unhealthy, stale, unknown, service-specific, load-related, safety-related, or context-limited. |
| Mission justification | A technically alive route can still be useless for the user; opaque health scores hide first failing gates and create wrong movement, rollback, or diagnosis. |
| Mission success criteria | Health evidence preserves producer, owner, timestamp, freshness, service, probe, raw result, normalized reason, confidence, context, and consumer path. |
| Mission failure criteria | Health collapses into a boolean/opaque score; missing evidence becomes pass; timeout becomes service failure without proof; reasons disappear between owners. |
| Mission boundaries | Owns structured health evidence. Does not own wake admission, incident creation, planning, authority, runtime execution, verification outcome, rollback, or operator primary action language. |
| Mission inputs | Observation facts, Reality Model context, service probes, quality, load, freshness, safety, route/readiness signals. |
| Mission outputs | Structured health evidence for Wake, Incident, Diagnosis, Decision Model, Planner, Authority, Verification, Engineering Reports. |
| Mission producers | Observation, service matrix, quality/readiness owners, diagnostics surfaces, state readers. |
| Mission consumers | Wake, Incident, Diagnosis, Decision Model, Planner, Authority, Runtime eligibility, Verification, Reports, Production Maturity. |
| Mission alternatives | Merge into Observation; merge into Decision Model; merge into Planner; treat as admin diagnostics only. |
| Separate-domain justification | Observation produces facts; Health Evidence structures suitability/health proof; downstream domains decide. This evidence layer is distinct and necessary. |
| Mission-structure conclusion | Separate domain justified. |

## Mission Success Model

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Mission Success Criteria | V7 can explain each health dimension and preserve raw/source evidence for downstream use. |
| Mission Failure Criteria | Opaque score, missing evidence as pass, timeout misclassification, lost reason/freshness/producer. |
| Mission Boundaries | Evidence only; no action owner or final verification result. |
| Mission Inputs | Observation, Reality Model, service/quality/load/freshness/safety signals. |
| Mission Outputs | Structured health evidence object or equivalent typed evidence set. |
| Mission Producers | Observation/service-matrix/quality/readiness owners. |
| Mission Consumers | Wake through Production Maturity. |

## Trusted Knowledge Produced

Health Evidence is the correct independent domain for structuring health proof. It must preserve source, target, service, freshness, load, safety, reason, producer, owner, probe, raw result, confidence, and consumer path where applicable. It explains suitability and risk, but does not become Wake, Incident, Planner, Authority, Runtime, or Verification outcome.

## V7 Project Expert Output

Inside V7, Health Evidence should explain the suitability of users, channels, services, sources, targets, and conditions. It supports decisions but does not become a primary action owner. ADR-003 keeps technical health diagnostics-only for operator UI, while Decision Model and downstream domains consume health/readiness/freshness/risk evidence.

## World Research Expert Output

Research strongly requires health as matrix evidence, not an opaque boolean. Every health decision should preserve producer, owner, timestamp, freshness, probe, reason, and consumer path. Missing or stale evidence must remain explicit.

## Implementation Expert Output

Function Graph contains distributed health/service/quality/readiness surfaces: 647 matched nodes, including mutation, read-only, and authority-marked consumers. This indicates Health Evidence is implementation-connected but not a single standalone runtime owner.

## Knowledge Synthesis

Health Evidence is an independent evidence-structuring domain. Current Domain 06 matches the ideal and correctly prevents health from becoming a decision, authority, or runtime action.

# Domain 07 — Intelligence

Intelligence

## Executive Summary

Domain analyzed: `07 Intelligence`.

Mission discovered: prepare production knowledge before action so the live execution path does not perform heavy investigation under pressure.

Ideal Domain Candidate: independent prepared-knowledge domain that builds evidence-derived, freshness-aware, advisory intelligence for downstream diagnosis, decision, planning, routing intelligence, engineering automation, and operator understanding.

What changed: architecture unchanged; trusted knowledge updated by certifying Intelligence as a background/advisory knowledge producer, not decision, authority, runtime, or production mutation owner.

Important gaps found: none blocking.

Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.

Recommended next action: complete this batch and proceed later to Domain 08 Routing Intelligence.

## Mission Discovery

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Fundamental engineering problem | V7 needs prepared knowledge before action: historical outcomes, service suitability, trust, prediction, recommendation context, and explainable advisory state must be available before a production decision or execution path needs them. |
| Mission justification | A production autonomous routing platform cannot perform expensive investigation or assemble all historical context inside the live mutation path. Prepared knowledge reduces pressure on Runtime and improves explainability without granting authority. |
| Mission success criteria | Downstream domains receive current, explainable, evidence-derived knowledge; advice remains non-authoritative; stale or weak knowledge cannot silently become production permission; real outcomes improve future recommendations. |
| Mission failure criteria | Advice becomes authority, stale knowledge is treated as live reality, synthetic or unverified evidence increases maturity, or background knowledge becomes hidden Planner/Runtime behavior. |
| Mission boundaries | Owns preparation, refresh, explanation, and advisory knowledge surfaces. Does not own product policy, formal decision semantics, Planner selection authority, Authority admission, Runtime apply, Verification result, Rollback, or production mutation. |
| Mission inputs | Reality Model, Observation, Health Evidence, service matrix, quality summaries, registries, historical audit records, governed feedback, rollback/switch records, learning closure, production outcomes. |
| Mission outputs | Intelligence snapshots, trust evolution summaries, prediction/readiness context, recommendation/advisory evidence, suitability context, engineering intelligence read models. |
| Mission producers | Observation, Health Evidence, Learning / Feedback, service matrix refresh, quality compact, governed execution closure, audit/history writers. |
| Mission consumers | Diagnosis, Decision Model, Planner, Routing Intelligence, operator/admin surfaces, Engineering Automation, OMP, Production Maturity. |
| Mission alternatives | Merge into Planner; merge into Learning; merge into Health Evidence; merge into Routing Intelligence; merge into Runtime. |
| Separate-domain justification | Planner consumes prepared knowledge but must not own all background investigation; Learning produces terminal outcome evidence but is narrower; Health Evidence structures health proof only; Routing Intelligence is specialized routing advice; Runtime must stay thin. Separate Intelligence is justified. |

Mission-structure conclusion: `Separate domain justified`.

## Mission Success Model

| Dimension | Success | Failure |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Preparedness | Knowledge is ready before live action. | Live execution performs heavy investigation under pressure. |
| Authority boundary | Advice remains advisory until downstream Authority/Runtime contracts accept it. | Advice silently becomes permission. |
| Evidence quality | Knowledge keeps source, freshness, confidence, and outcome basis. | Stale or synthetic knowledge is treated as production truth. |
| Downstream usefulness | Diagnosis, Decision Model, Planner, Routing Intelligence, OMP, and Production Maturity can consume explainable context. | Consumers receive opaque scores or untraceable recommendations. |
| Learning loop | Real outcomes improve future trust/recommendation quality. | Recommendations evolve from unsupported assumptions or blind operator agreement. |

## Second-Pass Review

Corpus repair note: this certification already performed second-pass contradiction review under the earlier heading `Second-Pass Contradiction Search` or `Mandatory Second-Pass Review`. The evidence is preserved and this canonical section name is restored for engine consistency.

## Ideal Domain Discovery

Given the mission, V7 needs a domain that prepares evidence-derived knowledge before action. The ideal solution is not a new planner, runtime, authority, or ML autopilot. It is an evidence preparation layer that:

- consumes real observations, health evidence, outcomes, feedback, and history;
- produces bounded, freshness-aware, explainable advisory knowledge;
- records confidence and source quality;
- feeds downstream decision and engineering consumers;
- cannot mutate production routing or grant authority by itself;
- can improve over time only from real validated outcomes.

| Name | Intelligence |
| Candidate summary | Independent prepared-knowledge domain for evidence-derived advisory intelligence. |
| Responsibilities | Build/update snapshots, trust summaries, prediction context, recommendation evidence, suitability context, and engineering intelligence read models. |
| Boundaries | No policy ownership, no final decision semantics, no Authority admission, no selected move ownership, no Runtime apply, no Verification result, no Rollback decision. |
| Inputs | Observation, Health Evidence, Learning, historical outcomes, audit records, service quality, registries, feedback closure. |
| Outputs | Prepared knowledge, advisory snapshots, explainable recommendation/trust/prediction/suitability evidence. |
| Producers | Observation/Health/Learning/Feedback/evidence owners. |
| Consumers | Diagnosis, Decision Model, Planner, Routing Intelligence, Engineering Automation, Admin UI, OMP, Production Maturity. |
| Greenfield Evidence | Runtime ADR, R1/R2/R3/R4 research, Production Maturity Engineering Intelligence, Function Graph snapshot/advisory implementation. |
| Domain existence | `KEEP_AS_SEPARATE_DOMAIN` |

## Trusted Knowledge

Intelligence is the V7 domain for prepared background knowledge. It turns real evidence, historical outcomes, feedback, trust, prediction, and service/suitability signals into explainable advisory state. It can write snapshots and read models, but those writes are not production routing mutations. It must remain downstream of evidence producers and upstream of diagnosis/decision/planner/advisory consumers. It must never become hidden authority, hidden planner, hidden Runtime, or synthetic maturity source.

## V7 Project Expert Output

Inside V7, Intelligence should be the prepared-knowledge domain. It consumes evidence and history, creates advisory/read-model knowledge, and feeds downstream consumers. It must not decide, authorize, execute, verify, rollback, or certify production capability by itself.

## World Research Expert Output

World research requires advisory/readiness/analyzer knowledge to be evidence-backed, owner-mapped, and backtested before it blocks or recommends mutation. Prepared knowledge should improve decisions and reduce pressure, but should not bypass authority or become direct action.

## Implementation Expert Output

Implementation contains connected intelligence snapshot and advisory surfaces: snapshot refresh, intelligence workers, routing brain advice, trust evolution, prediction/readiness helpers, and tests. The strongest implementation evidence says these surfaces are advisory/no-runtime-authority and write only snapshots/read models or support evidence.

## Knowledge Synthesis

The three realities agree. V7 wants prepared knowledge, research supports advisory/backtested intelligence, and implementation has connected snapshot/advisory surfaces. The current domain is correct and does not require architecture change.

| `C07-3` | Runtime consumes prepared knowledge. | Readiness should be outside critical path. | Planner loads snapshots/advice. | `NO` |

# Domain 08 — Routing Intelligence

Routing Intelligence

## Executive Summary

Domain analyzed: `08 Routing Intelligence`.

Mission discovered: give V7 route/user/service/target suitability knowledge before routing decisions, without turning advice into movement authority.

Ideal Domain Candidate: specialized advisory routing-knowledge domain that scores service fit, candidate suitability, pool context, trust, prediction, and explainability while Planner, Policy, Authority, Runtime, Verification, and Rollback keep their existing ownership.

What changed: architecture unchanged; trusted knowledge updated by certifying Routing Intelligence as a bounded advisory layer, not a hidden Planner or Authority.

Important gaps: none blocking.

Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.

Recommended next action: proceed to Domain 09 Wake.

## Mission Discovery

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Fundamental engineering problem | V7 must know which routes, targets, services, users, and pools are likely suitable before a routing decision, without collapsing that knowledge into a direct move command. |
| Mission justification | Production routing can harm users when target selection is based only on technical availability. V7 needs service fit, per-user priority, trust, pool health, load/capacity, and explainable suitability before Planner and Authority decide. |
| Mission success criteria | Routing advice improves candidate explanation and ranking; raw service/health/trust evidence remains inspectable; advice remains advisory; Planner hard gates, Policy, Authority, Runtime, Verification, and Rollback are not bypassed. |
| Mission failure criteria | Advisory score becomes action, stale suitability overrides live safety, service fit collapses into one opaque score, Routing Intelligence writes selected moves, or it moves users by itself. |
| Mission boundaries | Owns advisory route suitability, service/user fit, candidate/pool comparison, trust context, prediction context, and explanation. Does not own final action selection, policy, authority admission, selected move writes, identity lock, runtime apply, verification, or rollback. |
| Mission inputs | Intelligence snapshots, Health Evidence, service matrix, quality summary, user/service preferences, outcome history, trust/feedback records, Reality Model context. |
| Mission outputs | Candidate suitability advice, best-available-pool advice, service quality scores, weighted service fit, prediction/trust/blast-radius advice, explainability rows, bounded planner score parts. |
| Mission producers | Intelligence snapshot refresh, service matrix/quality owners, Learning/Feedback, routing intelligence read models, historical audit inputs. |
| Mission consumers | Decision Model, Planner, operator/admin read models, Production Maturity, OMP, and later Authority/Runtime only as downstream consumers of already-decided/gated state. |
| Mission alternatives | Merge into Intelligence; merge into Planner; merge into Health Evidence; merge into Authority; merge into Runtime. |
| Separate-domain justification | General Intelligence prepares broad knowledge, Health Evidence structures health proof, Planner selects moves, Authority admits execution. Routing Intelligence is the specialized routing suitability/advice layer between broad knowledge and decision consumers. |
| Mission-structure conclusion | `Separate domain justified` |

## Mission Success Model

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Mission Success Criteria | Advice is service-specific, user-aware, pool-aware, confidence/freshness-aware, inspectable, and consumed only as bounded advisory input after existing hard gates. |
| Mission Failure Criteria | Advice bypasses Planner/Policy/Authority, creates candidates directly, writes selected moves, hides raw evidence, or becomes runtime execution authority. |
| Mission Boundaries | Advisory routing knowledge only; no production mutation. |
| Mission Inputs | Service matrix, quality summary, preferences, audit/feedback/history, intelligence snapshots, health/readiness evidence. |
| Mission Outputs | Suitability score parts, pool advice, service quality/trend explanations, trust/prediction/blast advice. |
| Mission Producers | Health Evidence, Intelligence, Learning/Feedback, registry/read-model owners. |
| Mission Consumers | Decision Model, Planner, Admin UI, OMP/Production Maturity as evidence consumers. |

## Trusted Knowledge Produced

Routing Intelligence is the specialized V7 domain that turns service quality, user needs, target history, trust, prediction, pool context, and suitability evidence into bounded advisory routing knowledge. It may produce score parts, candidate suitability advice, and best-available-pool advice, but it must never create candidates directly, override hard gates, write selected moves, approve governance, mutate runtime state, or move users. Planner remains the decision owner; Authority remains the execution admission owner; Runtime remains the execution owner.

## V7 Project Expert Output

Inside V7, Routing Intelligence should be specialized advisory knowledge for route/service/user/target/pool suitability. It should help Decision Model and Planner understand candidates without becoming Planner, Policy, Authority, Runtime, or Verification.

## World Research Expert Output

Universal routing engineering principles require health and suitability to remain multi-dimensional, inspectable, capacity-aware, and authority-separated. Rerouting remains governed execution; score/advice is not execution.

## Implementation Expert Output

Implementation contains a connected Routing Intelligence surface: `admin_core.routing_intelligence`, `RoutingBrain`, candidate suitability, best available pool, service quality, prediction/trust advisory, and tests. The observed implementation repeatedly marks itself as advice-only with no selected move, governance, runtime, or execution authority.

## Knowledge Synthesis

All three realities agree: Routing Intelligence is a specialized advisory layer. Its architecture is not to move users, but to prepare route suitability knowledge for Planner/Decision consumers while preserving raw evidence and downstream gates.

# Domain 09 — Wake

Certification Engine: `V7_DOMAIN_ARCHITECTURE_CERTIFICATION_PROMPT.md`

Prompt Version: `1.0`

Status: `CANONICAL_PROMPT_LOCKED`

Execution Mode: `STRICT_SEQUENTIAL_DOMAIN_CERTIFICATION`

## Executive Summary

Domain 09 Wake analyzed.

Discovered mission: convert real, confirmed production evidence into a legal trigger for governed processing, while preventing timer, cron, blind polling, synthetic signals, and optimization signals from becoming production authority.

Ideal Domain Candidate: a trigger-legality domain between Health Evidence and Incident. It accepts only confirmed wake sources, rejects non-authoritative wake sources, preserves evidence provenance, and does not grant execution authority.

Current domain quality: `MATCHES_DISCOVERED_IDEAL`.

Important gaps found: `NONE`.

Architecture changed: `NO`.

Knowledge changed: `YES`.

Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.

Recommended next action: proceed to Domain 10 Incident.

## Mission Discovery

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Fundamental engineering problem | V7 needs a boundary that decides whether observed production evidence is strong enough to start governed reaction, without letting schedules or noise become authority. |
| Mission justification | Autonomous routing must react to real degradation, but a production movement chain cannot safely start merely because a timer fired or a probe exists. |
| Mission success criteria | Confirmed production failure can enter the governed L3 chain; unconfirmed, stale, synthetic, timer, cron, blind polling, and optimization-only triggers are rejected; downstream Incident receives an evidence-backed legal wake. |
| Mission failure criteria | Real degradation is ignored because no legal wake is materialized, or ordinary scheduling/noise starts governed recovery as if it were authority. |
| Mission boundaries | Wake owns trigger legality and accepted/rejected wake source evidence. It does not own diagnosis, incident lifecycle, planner ranking, authority admission, approved plan lock, restore barrier, runtime apply, verification, rollback, or learning. |
| Mission inputs | Health Evidence, Observation-derived current-channel failure evidence, service failure evidence, external wake events, freshness, wake policy. |
| Mission outputs | `v7.l3-wake-decision.v1`, accepted wake sources, rejected wake blockers, observed event consumption flags, evidence provenance for Incident. |
| Mission producers | Observation / Health Evidence writers, service matrix, current channel failure evidence producer, external wake event reader. |
| Mission consumers | Incident materialization, emergency failover authority gate, governed L3 chain diagnostics, reports. |
| Mission alternatives | Health Evidence, Incident, Authority, Runtime. |
| Separate-domain justification | Health Evidence records facts but should not decide trigger legality; Incident owns lifecycle after legal wake; Authority owns risk admission; Runtime executes approved objects. Wake is the necessary boundary between evidence and incident start. |

Mission-structure conclusion: `EXISTING DOMAIN IS CORRECT`.

## Mission Success Model

| Criterion | Expected result | Evidence quality |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Legal real failure wake | Confirmed service failure or confirmed current-channel failure can produce `ACCEPT_WAKE`. | HIGH |
| Timer rejection | `timer`, `cron`, `blind_polling`, `synthetic_wake`, `optimization_wake` do not become authority. | HIGH |
| No execution grant | Wake may allow governed processing to continue, but must not grant production mutation. | HIGH |
| Evidence provenance | Accepted wake includes source, channel, service/failure reason, generation, event id or inferred path. | HIGH |
| Downstream compatibility | Wake output feeds Incident without changing Runtime or Authority contracts. | HIGH |

## Three Independent Experts

### V7 Project Expert

Wake should be the legal trigger boundary. Inside V7 it must convert confirmed production evidence into an accepted wake source, reject blind scheduling/noise, and pass evidence to Incident without becoming authority or runtime execution.

### World Research Expert

Universal engineering principle: production systems separate detection, event/incident trigger legality, response authority, execution, verification, and rollback. Scheduling can initiate checks, but cannot itself prove that a production incident exists or that action is authorized.

### Implementation Expert

Implementation reality matches the boundary. `tools/v7-users-autoswitch` contains explicit allowed/rejected wake source policy, evidence production for current-channel failure, a wake decision function, consumed event tracking, and tests for both accepted confirmed failures and rejected timer sources.

## Second-Pass Review

Corpus repair note: this certification already performed second-pass contradiction review under the earlier heading `Second-Pass Contradiction Search` or `Mandatory Second-Pass Review`. The evidence is preserved and this canonical section name is restored for engine consistency.

## Ideal Domain Discovery

The ideal Wake domain should:

1. consume only evidence from Observation / Health Evidence / verified resume events;
2. normalize wake source vocabulary;
3. accept only confirmed legal sources;
4. reject timer, cron, blind polling, synthetic, stale, and optimization-only sources;
5. preserve provenance for Incident;
6. expose blockers when no legal wake exists;
7. fail closed;
8. never select actions, grant authority, mutate runtime state, verify, rollback, or close incidents.

## Ideal Domain Candidate

| Candidate field | Ideal responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| Domain name | Wake |
| Primary responsibility | Legalize or reject production wake sources before Incident. |
| Inputs | Confirmed service failure, confirmed current-channel failure, verified incident resume, recorded runtime resume, freshness, policy. |
| Outputs | Wake decision object, accepted sources, rejected blockers, observed events, consumed event ids, provenance. |
| Explicit non-ownership | No Planner ranking, no Authority budget, no Runtime apply, no Verification, no Rollback, no Learning. |
| Boundary | Between Health Evidence and Incident. |
| Failure behavior | Reject / STOP_SAFE with explicit blocker. |

## Current Domain Discovery

The current Domain 09 definition says Wake decides whether a confirmed production event is sufficient to start the next governed reaction cycle; it is not timer, cron, blind polling, or right-to-act authority. It receives Health Evidence and passes a recognized event to Incident. Its success/failure criteria match the ideal: real confirmed production problems can begin governed handling, while timer/polling/synthetic/unconfirmed signals cannot become authority.

## Domain Existence Review

Verdict: `KEEP`.

Wake should remain independent. Merging into Health Evidence would let fact recording also decide event legality. Merging into Incident would blur the boundary between trigger admission and incident lifecycle. Merging into Authority would make wake source legality part of risk budget approval. Merging into Runtime would make execution path responsible for deciding whether it should have been woken. None of those alternatives is cleaner or safer.

## Trusted Knowledge Produced

| Timer/cron/blind polling are rejected sources and cannot legalize L3 execution by themselves. | Policy constants; ADR; tests. | VERY HIGH |
| Wake and Incident are adjacent but distinct. | `_l3_wake_decision` versus `_l3_incident_key` / incident context; current domain text. | HIGH |
| Wake is allowed to expose blockers such as `confirmed_l3_wake_required` when legal evidence is absent. | Historical reports; code; tests. | HIGH |

# Domain 10 — Incident

Certification Engine: `V7_DOMAIN_ARCHITECTURE_CERTIFICATION_PROMPT.md`

## Executive Summary

Domain 10 Incident analyzed.

Discovered mission: preserve a confirmed production problem as a durable operational situation with identity, scope, affected users, source, lifecycle, and legal continuation/closure conditions.

Ideal Domain Candidate: an incident-lifecycle context domain after Wake and before Diagnosis/Planner. It owns incident identity, source/scope continuity, merge/split rules, affected reality, and lifecycle state. It does not own diagnosis details, action selection, authority admission, runtime apply, verification, rollback, learning, or final closure implementation.

Current domain quality: `MATCHES_DISCOVERED_IDEAL`.

Important gaps found: `NONE`.

Architecture changed: `NO`.

Knowledge changed: `YES`.

Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.

Recommended next action: proceed to Domain 11 Diagnosis.

## Mission Discovery

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Fundamental engineering problem | V7 needs to preserve a production failure as one stable operational object instead of treating repeated signals, selected moves, or individual actions as the problem itself. |
| Mission justification | Without incident identity and scope continuity, automation can move one user, forget remaining affected users, switch to unrelated optimization, or explain one execution with another execution object. |
| Mission success criteria | Incident key/source/scope/affected users survive across governed cycles; same-scope signals merge; different scopes split; remaining affected users keep the incident open; closure happens only under terminal proof. |
| Mission failure criteria | Incident source changes silently; affected users are lost; one-user success closes a still-active failed-source incident; unrelated users/sources are selected for the same incident; later owners consume another execution identity. |
| Mission boundaries | Incident owns operational situation identity, scope, source continuity, merge/split context, lifecycle state, affected users, and continuity evidence. It does not own root-cause diagnosis, ranking, authority budget, runtime mutation, verification, rollback, learning, or final capability update. |
| Mission inputs | Legal Wake decision, move evidence, failed sources/services, selected moves, planner generation, active runtime incident state, affected users, consumed wake events. |
| Mission outputs | `v7.l3-incident-context.v1`, `v7.l3-runtime-incident-record.v1`, incident key, incident source, affected users, lifecycle state, merge/split/event-collapse context, runtime lifecycle record. |
| Mission producers | Wake, emergency failover authority gate, failed-source scope reader, L3 runtime state reader. |
| Mission consumers | Diagnosis, Planner continuation, Authority/Approved Plan Lock, Runtime eligibility/apply, Learning/Closure, Current Program State, operator visibility. |
| Mission alternatives | Wake, Diagnosis, Planner, Authority, Runtime, Rollback / Closure. |
| Separate-domain justification | Wake only admits legal triggers; Diagnosis explains why; Planner selects actions; Authority admits risk; Runtime executes; Closure records terminal outcomes. Incident is the only clean owner for stable operational situation identity and scope continuity between those domains. |

Mission-structure conclusion: `EXISTING DOMAIN IS CORRECT`.

| --- | --- | --- |
| Incident materialization | Confirmed current-channel/service failure with affected users produces incident context. | HIGH |
| Identity continuity | Incident key/source/source scope remain stable across cycles when the same failed-source incident remains active. | HIGH |
| Merge/split correctness | Same scope merges; materially different source/service/authority/generation splits. | HIGH |
| Remaining affected users | One successful move does not close the incident while source remains failed and affected users remain. | HIGH |
| Downstream identity protection | Approved Plan Lock rejects selected moves whose source does not match incident source during continuation. | HIGH |
| No execution ownership | Incident exposes context and lifecycle state but does not grant authority or execute. | HIGH |
