## Three Independent Experts

### V7 Project Expert

Inside V7, Incident should be the durable operational situation object. It materializes legal Wake evidence into identity/scope/context, preserves failed-source continuity, carries affected users and lifecycle state, and protects downstream owners from switching execution identity.

### World Research Expert

World-class operational systems do not act on raw alerts as isolated events. They create incident objects with scope, impact, lifecycle, ownership, escalation/containment, merge/split behavior, and terminal learning. The universal principle is durable incident identity before response execution.

### Implementation Expert

Implementation reality supports Incident as a concrete L3 context and runtime record. The key functions materialize incident context, reuse or split keys, preserve incident_source, feed runtime lifecycle, expose operator surface, and keep incidents open when affected users remain. Unit tests cover the main historical failure modes.

## Second-Pass Review

## Ideal Domain Discovery

The ideal Incident domain should:

1. consume only legal Wake events and related evidence;
2. create or update a stable incident identity;
3. preserve failed source, affected users, failed services, authority envelope, generation, selected move hash, and operation id;
4. merge duplicate same-scope events;
5. split materially different scopes;
6. keep the incident open while source remains failed and affected users remain;
7. prevent unrelated optimization/action from replacing the incident;
8. expose lifecycle state and next-action context;
9. let downstream owners diagnose, plan, authorize, execute, verify, rollback, learn, and close.

## Ideal Domain Candidate

| Candidate field | Ideal responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| Domain name | Incident |
| Primary responsibility | Durable production situation identity and scope continuity. |
| Inputs | Legal Wake decision, failure evidence, affected users, selected move context, runtime incident state, authority envelope, generation. |
| Outputs | Incident context, incident key, incident source, affected users, lifecycle status, merge/split/event-collapse evidence, runtime incident record. |
| Explicit non-ownership | No root-cause diagnosis, no action ranking, no authority admission, no Runtime apply, no verification, no rollback, no final learning ownership. |
| Boundary | After Wake; before Diagnosis/Planner/Authority/Runtime consumers. |
| Failure behavior | STOP_SAFE / keep-open / split / suspend when scope cannot be safely owned. |

## Current Domain Discovery

The current Domain 10 defines Incident as an оформленная production-ситуация where a confirmed problem has scope, affected reality, and reason for governed continuation. It explicitly says Incident is not one signal and not a selected action; it preserves context until recovery, containment, canonical impossibility, or legal closure. It owns incident identity, affected scope, incident source, continuation and closure conditions, but not diagnosis details, planning, authority, execution, verification, or learning outcome.

## Domain Existence Review

Verdict: `KEEP`.

Incident must remain a separate domain. Wake cannot own durable lifecycle because it only admits triggers. Diagnosis cannot own incident identity because it explains causes after context exists. Planner cannot own it because action selection would bias scope. Authority cannot own it because risk admission is not the incident itself. Runtime cannot own it because execution should consume an already-identified operational object. Rollback / Closure cannot own it because closure is the end of incident lifecycle, not the whole lifecycle.

# Domain 11 — Diagnosis

Certification Engine: `V7_DOMAIN_ARCHITECTURE_CERTIFICATION_PROMPT.md`

Prompt Version: `1.0`

Status: `CANONICAL_PROMPT_LOCKED`

Execution Mode: `STRICT_SEQUENTIAL_DOMAIN_CERTIFICATION`

## Executive Summary

Domain 11 Diagnosis analyzed.

Discovered mission: convert incident/evidence state into a proven explanation of cause, owner, condition, unknown state, policy boundary, or required continuation without confusing symptoms with root cause.

Ideal Domain Candidate: a read-only, evidence-preserving diagnostic domain between Incident and Decision Model / Planner / Authority. It must classify root cause, blocking owner, unknown, policy boundary, and first divergence; it must route owner blocks into Owner Resolution; it must not select actions, grant authority, execute, verify, or mutate production.

Current domain quality: `CLOSE_TO_DISCOVERED_IDEAL`.

Important gaps found: `IMPLEMENTATION_GAP`, `OWNERSHIP_GAP`.

Architecture changed: `NO`.

Knowledge changed: `YES`.

Certification result: `NOT CERTIFIED`.

Recommended next action: create an owner-mapped implementation mission for executable Diagnosis / Owner Resolution projection before treating this domain as fully certified.

## Mission Discovery

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Fundamental engineering problem | V7 must explain why a symptom or incident exists and who owns the condition before downstream domains treat the evidence as a decision basis. |
| Mission justification | Without Diagnosis, V7 can misclassify service symptoms as source failure, stale evidence as target failure, unknown as fail/pass, or an owner block as terminal truth. |
| Mission success criteria | Root cause, owner, first divergence, unknown state, missing evidence, implementation defect, policy prohibition, or canonical impossibility are classified with evidence and source object; no blocker label is accepted as final explanation. |
| Mission failure criteria | First blocker becomes root cause; symptoms are treated as causes; unknown is masked as fail/pass; reports explain another execution object; owner block remains unresolved. |
| Mission boundaries | Diagnosis owns explanation/classification and owner-resolution routing. It does not own action ranking, authority admission, runtime execution, verification result production, rollback, or production mutation. |
| Mission inputs | Incident context, Health Evidence, Observation evidence, blockers, source object pointers, freshness, execution identity, engineering reports, artifacts, owner map. |
| Mission outputs | Proven diagnosis, owner resolution state, root cause classification, first divergence, unknown classification, required resolution, evidence pointers, confidence/backtesting status for analyzers. |
| Mission producers | Incident, Health Evidence, Observation, Runtime/Verification blockers, Engineering Reports, Owner Resolution evidence. |
| Mission consumers | Decision Model, Planner, Authority, OMP, Current Program State, Production Maturity, Engineering Automation. |
| Mission alternatives | Incident, Decision Model, Planner, Authority, Engineering Reports, OMP. |
| Separate-domain justification | Incident preserves situation; Decision Model structures possible choices; Planner selects; Authority admits; reports preserve evidence. Diagnosis is the missing interpretation boundary that proves what condition actually exists before those consumers act. |

Mission-structure conclusion: `EXISTING DOMAIN IS CORRECT`, with implementation maturity gap.

| Object continuity | Diagnosis explains the same incident/execution object. | HIGH |
| Read-only analyzer discipline | Automated diagnosis remains read-only and backtested before blocking or mutation. | HIGH |
| Executable owner closure | A concrete owner can produce diagnosis/owner-resolution outputs without permanent manual/Codex dependency. | MEDIUM / PARTIAL |

## Three Independent Experts

### V7 Project Expert

Diagnosis should be the evidence-preserving explanation boundary: it must distinguish symptom from cause, classify owner/root condition/unknown/policy/implementation defect, and route blockers into Owner Resolution. V7 canonical docs require it, but current implementation is not yet a single closed executable owner.

### World Research Expert

Universal principle: detection, triage, diagnosis/RCA, response, verification, rollback, and learning are separate stages. Automated diagnosis can reduce MTTR only if analyzers are read-only, backtested, owner-routed, and prevented from becoming unverified authority.

### Implementation Expert

Implementation reality is partial. Diagnostic evidence, blockers, status helpers, reports, and read models exist, but the Function Graph and Knowledge Consolidation explicitly show Diagnosis / Owner Resolution as partially closed, with no single executable diagnosis owner or full owner-resolution loop.

## Second-Pass Review

## Ideal Domain Discovery

The ideal Diagnosis domain should:

1. consume Incident context and evidence without changing it;
2. preserve execution object identity;
3. separate symptom, cause, blocker, missing evidence, policy boundary, and unknown;
4. prove first divergence and producer/consumer/owner when relevant;
5. classify owner blocks into terminal Owner Resolution states;
6. expose required resolution and reason no further investigation is necessary;
7. remain read-only until analyzer backtesting/certification proves blocking value;
8. feed Decision Model, Authority, OMP, CPS, Production Maturity and Engineering Automation.

## Ideal Domain Candidate

| Candidate field | Ideal responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| Domain name | Diagnosis |
| Primary responsibility | Evidence-backed cause/owner/unknown/policy classification for incident state. |
| Inputs | Incident, Health Evidence, Observation, blockers, artifacts, source objects, freshness, owner map, historical reports. |
| Outputs | Diagnosis record, root cause classification, owner resolution state, first divergence, missing evidence classification, required resolution, analyzer confidence. |
| Explicit non-ownership | No Planner ranking, no Authority admission, no Runtime apply, no Verification result production, no Rollback, no production mutation. |
| Boundary | After Incident; before Decision Model / Planner / Authority / OMP consumption. |
| Failure behavior | Preserve unknown or route Owner Resolution; never convert blocker label into terminal explanation. |

## Current Domain Discovery

The current Domain 11 text defines Diagnosis as the domain that explains why observed or incident state occurred, which owner or condition caused it, and what is proven. It explicitly says Diagnosis is not a decision or authority. It owns root-cause classification, owner resolution, symptom/cause distinction, unknown, and explanation of why investigation can stop or must continue. This matches the ideal architecture.

## Domain Existence Review

Verdict: `KEEP`.

Diagnosis must remain independent. Incident preserves the situation; Diagnosis explains it. Decision Model structures choices after diagnosis; Planner selects action candidates; Authority admits risk; Runtime executes; OMP routes work. Merging Diagnosis into any of these would either hide root-cause uncertainty or let a consumer become the producer of truth it consumes.

# Domain 12 — Decision Model

## Executive Summary

Domain 12 Decision Model recovered from missing certification evidence and certified.

Mission discovered: define the structured language, allowed outcomes, required inputs, escalation semantics, and decision/execution separation for V7 decisions.

Ideal Domain Candidate: documentation-only read model over existing owners that turns product intent, current/desired state, evidence, policy, risk, authority need, verification expectation, and learning path into a shared decision vocabulary.

Current domain quality: `MATCHES_DISCOVERED_IDEAL`.

Important gaps: none blocking.

Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.

Recommended next action: proceed to Domain 18 Execution after persistence verification.

## Mission Discovery

Decision Model exists to prevent raw signal, score, diagnosis, operator preference, timer, or planner convenience from becoming an implicit production action.

The fundamental engineering problem is semantic separation: V7 must know what kind of decision it is making before any owner may plan, authorize, execute, verify, roll back, or learn from it.

If this domain disappeared, action vocabulary would drift across Policy, Planner, Authority, Runtime, reports and operator surfaces. A `MOVE`, `FAILOVER`, `NO_ACTION`, `ASK_OPERATOR`, `STOP_SAFE` or `PROBE_ONLY` could become owner-local language instead of one coherent product decision model.

Separate domain justified: yes. Policy owns rules, Planner selects a candidate, Authority admits action scope, Runtime executes/stops, but Decision Model owns the shared semantics that all of them consume.

## Mission Success Model

Mission success criteria:

- every V7 decision can be explained through current state, desired state, policy basis, evidence basis, risk, authority need, verification expectation and learning path;
- decision vocabulary remains shared across operator surfaces, Policy, Planner, Authority, Runtime, Verification, Rollback and Learning;
- escalation and no-action outcomes are treated as valid decisions, not failures;
- decision remains separate from execution.

Mission failure criteria:

- scores or diagnostics become actions;
- Planner labels become execution truth;
- Policy, Authority or Runtime create competing decision vocabularies;
- decision output cannot be verified or learned from.

Mission boundaries:

- input: product intent, current/desired state, evidence, diagnosis, intelligence, policy and risk;
- output: structured decision semantics and allowed decision vocabulary;
- producers: Business Objective, Product Principles, Reality Model, Health Evidence, Diagnosis and Intelligence;
- consumers: Policy, Planner, Authority, Runtime-facing read models, operator surfaces, Verification, Rollback and Learning;
- not owned: planning implementation, authority grant, identity lock, runtime apply, verification, rollback or learning storage.

## Second-Pass Review

Contradictory evidence searched:

- no source requires a new Decision Model owner;
- no source allows Planner decision labels to bypass Authority or Runtime;
- no source treats Decision Model as execution permission;
- no source shows Domain 12 certification already fully persisted.

Recovered evidence changes the corpus state from `MISSING` to `COMPLETE` for Domain 12.

## Ideal Domain Discovery

An ideal Decision Model domain for V7 should:

- define common decision vocabulary;
- separate current state, desired state, policy, evidence, risk, authority, verification and learning inputs;
- treat escalation and no-action as valid outcomes;
- remain documentation/read-model semantics, not runtime mutation;
- be consumed by Policy, Planner, Authority, Runtime, Verification, Rollback and Learning.

## Ideal Domain Candidate

Decision Model is the shared semantic contract for V7 decisions. It defines what a decision means, what inputs must be visible, what outcomes are allowed, when escalation is valid, how authority and verification expectations are represented, and why choosing an action is not executing it.

It must be a documentation-only/read-model domain over existing owners. It must not create a new planner, authority, runtime, truth source, storage path, synthetic evidence source or movement authority.

## Current Domain Discovery

The current Domain 12 in Knowledge Consolidation defines Decision Model as the language and form of V7 decisions. It owns decision vocabulary, inputs, escalation semantics, stop outcomes, reconciliation between current/desired state and explainability. It explicitly excludes planning implementation, authority approval, identity lock, runtime apply, verification and learning storage.

| Consumers | Policy/Planner/Authority/Runtime/explanations. | Same. | MATCH |
| Owner need | No new owner. | Canonical docs say Need New Owner FALSE. | MATCH |

## Architectural Equivalence

`EQUIVALENT`

Current Domain 12 and the discovered ideal describe the same architectural responsibility. Differences are wording/detail only, not architectural.

## Domain Existence Review

Separate domain justified.

Decision semantics cannot be merged into Policy because policy constrains decisions; it cannot be merged into Planner because Planner selects candidates; it cannot be merged into Authority because Authority grants admission; it cannot be merged into Runtime because Runtime executes or stops live action.

## Trusted Knowledge

- Decision Model is a canonical documentation-only/read model.
- Decision Model owns decision semantics, not production mutation.
- Decision vocabulary is a shared interface consumed by downstream owners.
- Escalation, no-action and probe-only are valid decisions.
- Existing owners are sufficient; no new Decision owner is required.

# Domain 13 — Policy

## Executive Summary

Domain 13 Policy проанализирован как отдельный архитектурный домен.

Обнаруженная миссия: переводить product intent, бизнес-риск и системные законы в operational boundaries до того, как Planner, Authority или Runtime смогут рассматривать действие как допустимое.

Ideal Domain Candidate: независимый rule-source домен, который задает constraints, guardrails, fail-open/fail-closed semantics, freshness, blast radius, rollback, exception and risk boundaries, но не выбирает, не одобряет и не исполняет production action.

Текущий домен соответствует найденному идеалу.

Важная оговорка: некоторые отдельные policy-документы еще находятся в lifecycle `V7_FIT_ANALYSIS_COMPLETE_IMPLEMENTATION_BACKLOG_READY`, но это не архитектурный дефект домена Policy; это нормальная lifecycle зрелость отдельных policy families.

Блокирующих gaps не найдено.

Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.

Recommended next action: перейти к Domain 14 Planner.

## Mission Discovery

Policy существует, чтобы V7 не превращал техническую возможность в разрешенное production-действие. Фундаментальная инженерная проблема домена: между бизнесовым намерением и действием должна существовать явная граница допустимости. Без Policy Runtime или Planner могли бы использовать health score, route availability, service evidence или automation trigger как скрытое разрешение на действие, хотя реальный риск, authority scope, blast radius, freshness, rollback, anti-flap, exception and human boundary еще не подтверждены.

Для production autonomous routing platform эта проблема критична: V7 работает с реальными пользователями, маршрутами, каналами, сервисами и восстановлением доступа. Ошибка Policy может не просто выбрать плохой route; она может расширить blast radius, нарушить business risk appetite, автоматизировать запрещенную exception, использовать stale evidence или запустить действие без rollback/verification boundary.

Эта миссия не должна принадлежать Planner, Authority или Runtime. Planner выбирает candidate внутри уже существующих ограничений. Authority проверяет, разрешено ли продолжить выбранное намерение внутри текущего certified scope. Runtime исполняет или останавливает уже подготовленный и допущенный объект. Policy является rule source, а не admission gate и не execution engine.

Если Policy исчезнет, V7 потеряет явный перевод между Business Objective and operational rules. Product wishes начнут просачиваться напрямую в execution logic, policy duplication появится в downstream owners, exception boundaries станут неявными, а safety поведение станет неоднозначным.

World-class системный принцип: policy decision should be separated from enforcement; humans retain business/policy/exception ownership; risky automation requires explicit guardrails, bounded blast radius, freshness, rollback and escalation semantics. V7 intent совпадает с этим: Canonical Policy Library, Decision Model Law 2, Product Specification Policy Translation and ADR-V7 Canonical Policy Library закрепляют Policy как отдельный source of operational behavior rules.

## Mission Success Model

| Item | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Mission Success Criteria | Технически возможное действие не может стать safe/eligible/approved без прохождения approved policy constraints: authority, blast radius, freshness, rollback/no-rollback, anti-flap, service/user/SLA fit, exception and safety boundaries. |
| Mission Failure Criteria | Raw product wishes или raw health/evidence становятся direct execution input; policy logic дублируется между owners; exception boundaries скрыты; fail-open/fail-closed semantics неоднозначны; Runtime или Planner изобретают policy под давлением. |
| Mission Boundaries | Policy определяет allowed/blocked/exception boundaries. Он не делает observation, не выбирает candidate, не расширяет authority, не lock-ит identity, не исполняет apply, не верифицирует outcome и не закрывает rollback. |
| Mission Inputs | Business Objective, Product Principles, System Laws, Decision Model, world research, canonical policy lifecycle, current production maturity evidence. |
| Mission Outputs | Operational rules, policy constraints, policy windows, risk boundaries, exception rules, fail-open/fail-closed behavior, safety/rollback/freshness/blast-radius requirements. |
| Mission Producers | Product Specification, Canonical Policy Library, ADR-V7 Canonical Policy Library, OMP policy lifecycle, research-backed policy documents. |
| Mission Consumers | Planner, Authority, Runtime, Execution, Verification, Rollback / Closure, OMP, Production Maturity, Current Program State, operator surfaces. |

## Three Independent Experts

### V7 Project Expert

Policy inside V7 should be the durable translation layer from product/business meaning into operational constraints. It should own rule source semantics and lifecycle, not execution. The canonical project view is consistent: Product Specification says Business Objectives become Canonical Policies; Decision Model says policy constraints must be evaluated before action is exposed as safe; ADR-V7 Canonical Policy Library makes `docs/policies/` the permanent source for operational behavior policy; Knowledge Consolidation positions Policy after Decision Model and before Planner.

The project evidence also proves Policy must stay separate from Authority. Policy defines boundaries; Authority admits or refuses a candidate inside current certified scope and risk level. Merging them would make policy source and action admission the same owner.

### World Research Expert

Universal engineering principles for this domain are stable:

1. Policy decision and enforcement should be separable.
2. Automation may act inside pre-approved boundaries but must not expand those boundaries itself.
3. Humans retain business policy, exception, and architecture responsibility.
4. Risky production actions require explicit freshness, rollback/containment, blast-radius and safety boundaries.
5. Fail-open/fail-closed behavior must be explicit and action-class dependent.
6. Progressive rollout, safety halt, rollback and circuit-breaker behavior are policy-shaped controls, not incidental implementation details.

These principles support a separate Policy domain.

### Implementation Expert

Implementation reality shows distributed policy consumption rather than a single policy engine. `tools/v7-users-autoswitch` loads `policy.json` and org policy and derives concrete policy surfaces consumed by planning/gating: switch policy, authority budget policy, emergency failover policy, quality/load/reconnect/safety/service policies and pool policy. `tools/v7-autoswitch-safety-review` evaluates policy bounds. `tools/v7-runtime-contract-validate` validates policy file shape. Function Graph Appendix and JSON confirm policy-relevant nodes, downstream consumers, tests and read-only/advisory projections.

This implementation shape is compatible with the domain: Policy is the rule source and downstream owners enforce or consume the constraints.

## Second-Pass Review

Contradictory evidence was checked.

Potential contradiction: individual policy documents such as POLICY_001, POLICY_004, POLICY_006, POLICY_007 and POLICY_008 include lifecycle markers like `V7_FIT_ANALYSIS_COMPLETE_IMPLEMENTATION_BACKLOG_READY`, `RESEARCH_PENDING`, `NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, and `Runtime automation enabled: NO`.

Resolution: this does not invalidate the Policy domain. It means several policy families are not fully operationalized for all action classes. The domain's architecture, source ownership, lifecycle and downstream consumption are still established. Certification concerns the domain architecture, not completion of every policy-family implementation backlog item.

Potential contradiction: Function Graph classifies some policy-adjacent nodes as mutation-capable.

Resolution: these nodes are support/governance or runtime-adjacent entrypoints that require owner/authority for execution changes; this supports rather than weakens the Policy boundary. Policy itself does not grant mutation authority.

No unresolved conflict remains.

## Ideal Domain Discovery

The ideal Policy domain should:

- translate product/business intent into operational constraints;
- be explicit enough for downstream owners to enforce without redoing product reasoning;
- preserve human ownership of policy, exceptions, and architecture boundaries;
- express safety, risk, freshness, rollback, blast radius, anti-flap, service/user/SLA fit and fail-open/fail-closed semantics;
- feed Planner, Authority and Runtime without becoming any of them;
- evolve through research, V7 fit analysis, existing-owner reuse, implementation, verification, certification and OMP integration.

It should not select candidates, approve scope, execute production actions, verify outcomes, close incidents or learn maturity from results.

## Ideal Domain Candidate

Policy is an independent rule-source domain that converts Business Objective, Product Principles, System Laws, Decision Model and validated research into operational boundaries. It defines what is allowed, forbidden, risky, exceptional, stale, reversible, bounded, fail-closed, fail-open, or human-required. Its output is consumed by Planner, Authority, Runtime and certification/maturity owners. It remains documentation/source-of-truth plus policy-derived implementation surfaces, not a new Runtime, Planner, Authority or execution path.

## Current Domain Discovery

The current Domain 13 text defines Policy as translating product intent and business objectives into operational boundaries. It says Policy determines permitted actions, blockers, acceptable risk and exceptions requiring human decision. It owns constraints, guardrails, fail-open/fail-closed semantics, SLA/service/user fit, risk appetite and exception boundaries, while explicitly excluding observations, candidate selection, authority expansion, runtime execution and verification.

The current text also places Policy after Decision Model and before Planner, and states consumers include Planner, Authority, Runtime and certification domains.

## Architectural Equivalence

`EQUIVALENT`

Corpus repair note: structural comparison and certification verdict already established equivalence or a non-blocking implementation mission. This canonical section name is restored without changing the architectural conclusion.

## Domain Existence Review

Verdict: `KEEP`.

Policy must remain a separate domain. Merging it into Decision Model would hide operational boundaries inside decision semantics. Merging it into Planner would let a candidate selector own the rules it must obey. Merging it into Authority would confuse rule definition with action admission. Merging it into Runtime would violate thin-runtime and make product policy part of live execution. Merging it into OMP would make the program manager the policy source rather than the policy lifecycle consumer.

# Domain 14 — Planner

Prompt Status: `CANONICAL_PROMPT_LOCKED`

Certification Date: 2026-07-06

Certification Result: `CERTIFIED`

## Executive Summary

Domain 14 Planner был сертифицирован как самостоятельный архитектурный домен V7.

Обнаруженная миссия: превращать доказанную production-ситуацию, evidence, policy и ограничения в bounded candidate / selected move или явный stop outcome.

Ideal Domain Candidate: Planner является owner выбора кандидатов и selected moves, но не является Authority, Runtime, Verification, Rollback или execution owner.

Текущий домен совпадает с discovered ideal.

Главное уточнение: техническая реализация находится в `tools/v7-users-autoswitch`, где рядом существуют planning и apply-пути, но архитектурная ответственность Planner заканчивается на candidate / selected move / no-action output.

Блокирующих architecture, ownership, runtime, authority, mutation или closure gaps не найдено.

Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.

Recommended next action: перейти к Domain 15 Authority.

## Mission Discovery

Planner существует, чтобы V7 не превращал наблюдение или диагноз напрямую в production mutation. Его инженерная задача — построить доказуемое, ограниченное и объяснимое намерение действия: кого можно двигать, откуда, куда, почему, в каком классе действия, с каким selected move identity, и почему безопасного candidate может не быть.

Фундаментальная проблема, которую решает домен: в автономной routing platform всегда существует разрыв между "реальность требует реакции" и "можно безопасно выполнить конкретное действие". Planner заполняет этот разрыв не исполнением, а выбором bounded candidate на основе текущей реальности, incident context, health/service evidence, policy, load, quality, safety, retry history, identity and blast-radius constraints.

Если Planner исчезнет, V7 либо начнет действовать реактивно без структурированного выбора, либо переложит выбор на Runtime, Authority или Policy. Это нарушит Decision Is Not Execution, Owner Separation и Object Continuity.

Отдельный домен оправдан: Policy определяет правила, Diagnosis объясняет причину, Authority допускает scope, Runtime исполняет или STOP_SAFE, Verification проверяет outcome. Ни один из этих доменов не должен ранжировать и выбирать candidate moves.

## Mission Success Model

| Aspect | Model |
| --- | --- |
| Mission Success Criteria | Planner produces an explainable candidate/selected move or explicit stop/no-action using only available evidence and policy. |
| Mission Failure Criteria | Planner invents execution truth, bypasses policy/authority/runtime, selects unrelated incident scope, repeats exhausted semantic attempts, hides blockers, or mutates production. |
| Mission Boundaries | Owns candidate selection, ranking, blockers, selected move identity and explanation. Does not own authority, committed lock, apply, verification, rollback, learning or closure. |
| Mission Inputs | Incident/diagnosis context, affected users, current assignments, health evidence, service matrix, quality/load state, route class, policy, safety state, retry history, authority budget as constraint. |
| Mission Outputs | Decisions, candidates, blockers, selected moves, selected move hash, explanations, no-action/stop outcome, planner generation/operation identity. |
| Mission Producers | Observation, Health Evidence, Wake, Incident, Diagnosis, Decision Model, Policy and Routing Intelligence provide upstream context. |
| Mission Consumers | Authority, Approved Plan Lock, Restore Barrier, Runtime, Verification, Rollback, Learning, OMP and operator surfaces consume Planner output. |

## Three Independent Experts

### V7 Project Expert

Inside V7, Planner should be the domain that converts an actionable incident/evidence/policy state into an explicit candidate selection or stop/no-action. It must not own authority admission, runtime execution, verification, rollback, learning or closure. The project evidence is consistent: Decision Model defines Prepared Plan as advisory, Runtime Model consumes bounded candidates, L3 capability assigns affected-user and target selection to Planner, and SYSTEM_MAP places Planner in the Planning Plane.

### World Research Expert

Universal engineering principles support a separate planning layer. A reliable autonomous control system separates observation, diagnosis, planning, admission, execution, verification and learning. The planner must rank and filter possible actions using health, service fitness, capacity, policy, risk, blast radius, hysteresis, retry history and identity continuity. It must not execute the action it proposes, and it must produce evidence that downstream gates can verify.

### Implementation Expert

Implementation reality shows an existing Planner surface in `tools/v7-users-autoswitch`. The `AutoswitchPlanner.plan` path builds per-user decisions, applies incident_source filtering, selects moves, respects authority/request caps, validates approved plan lock context, and produces selected move identity. `_candidate` and its gates produce eligibility, blockers, reasons and scores. `_pick_projected_moves` excludes retry-exhausted semantic attempts and applies projected capacity constraints. Apply-related functions exist in the same tool, but their responsibility is downstream execution rather than Planner architecture.

## Second-Pass Review

Contradictory evidence was checked.

Potential contradiction: `tools/v7-users-autoswitch` contains both planning and apply behavior, and Function Graph lists `AutoswitchPlanner.apply`.

Resolution: this is implementation colocation, not architectural merger. Canonical documents say Runtime executes or STOP_SAFE and Planner selects candidate. The apply path consumes approved plan lock, restore barrier and runtime eligibility; it does not make Planner the owner of Runtime.

Potential contradiction: historical production defects included wrong/unstable Planner behavior around incident_source, retry-exhausted attempts and selected-move continuity.

Resolution: those reports show the correct owner for the defects and the existing correction path. Later evidence and tests show incident_source continuity, retry-aware candidate filtering and governed batch selection are implemented through existing Planner/autoswitch owners.

Potential contradiction: Routing Intelligence and operator decision surfaces can influence candidates.

Resolution: they are advisory/consumer surfaces. Planner remains the owner that turns available evidence and advice into selected moves or blockers.

No unresolved conflict remains.

## Ideal Domain Discovery

The ideal Planner domain should:

- receive current production reality, incident scope, health evidence, policy and decision context;
- build candidate targets and user-level decisions without mutating production;
- explicitly block unsafe, stale, overloaded, forbidden, exhausted or unrelated candidates;
- rank candidates only inside the active incident/product scope;
- preserve user/source/target/action/generation/selected move identity for downstream owners;
- produce selected moves, selected move hash, reasons, blockers and no-action/stop outcomes;
- remain bounded by Authority, Restore Barrier, Runtime, Verification and Rollback;
- never silently replace a committed plan after execution identity is locked.

## Ideal Domain Candidate

Planner is an independent candidate-selection domain. It transforms evidence-backed incident and decision context into bounded, explainable, identity-stable candidate actions or explicit stop/no-action outcomes. It owns candidate eligibility, blockers, ranking, selected move construction and selected move explanation. It does not grant authority, create execution packets by itself, bypass restore barrier, run apply, verify results, rollback, close incidents or learn maturity.

## Current Domain Discovery

Current Domain 14 defines Planner as the domain that converts proven situation and constraints into candidate action or stop outcome. It says Planner chooses an allowable direction based on current reality, desired product state, policy constraints, health evidence, risk, blast radius and readiness, while explicitly stating Planner does not execute production action.

The current domain assigns selection/refusal responsibility to Planner and excludes authority admission, committed identity lock, runtime execution, verification and rollback closure.

## Structural Comparison

| Dimension | Ideal Domain Candidate | Current Domain | Result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| Execution boundary | Does not execute production action. | Explicitly stated. | `MATCH` |
| Authority boundary | Does not grant authority/admission. | Explicitly excluded. | `MATCH` |
| Identity continuity | Must preserve selected move identity. | Covered through Object Continuity and supporting evidence. | `MATCH` |
| Incident scope | Must avoid unrelated source/action and exhausted attempts. | Success/failure criteria include unrelated/exhausted action. | `MATCH` |
| Downstream consumers | Authority, lock, barrier, Runtime, verification/rollback. | Authority named; runtime/verification/rollback excluded as owned elsewhere. | `COMPATIBLE` |
| Implementation reality | Existing autoswitch Planner implements candidate and selected move path. | Domain does not overclaim implementation. | `MATCH` |

Architectural Equivalence: `ARCHITECTURE_EQUIVALENT`.

## Architectural Equivalence

`EQUIVALENT`

Corpus repair note: structural comparison and certification verdict already established equivalence or a non-blocking implementation mission. This canonical section name is restored without changing the architectural conclusion.

## Domain Existence Review

Verdict: `KEEP`.

Planner must remain a separate domain. Merging it into Decision Model would turn abstract decision semantics into active candidate selection. Merging it into Policy would let rules select their own actions. Merging it into Authority would collapse candidate selection and admission. Merging it into Runtime would violate Decision Is Not Execution and create hidden replanning inside execution. Merging it into Routing Intelligence would turn advisory scoring into action selection.

# Domain 15 — Authority

Authority

## Executive Summary

Domain analyzed: 15 Authority.

Mission discovered: отделять право продолжить production action от evidence, plan, technical possibility и runtime safety.

Ideal Domain Candidate: отдельный authority/admission boundary, который ограничивает action class, scope, blast radius, risk, lifecycle state, delegated policy and operator approval.

What changed: архитектура V7 не изменилась; доверенное знание обновлено в части различия между Authority domain implementation и текущей low-maturity Authority Evolution state.

Important gaps: blocking gaps не найдено; POLICY_004 / delegated autonomy lifecycle remains incomplete, но это non-blocking lifecycle work через существующих owners, не архитектурный дефект домена.

Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.

Recommended next action: перейти к Domain 16 Identity.

## Mission Discovery

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Fundamental engineering problem | V7 нужен отдельный слой, который отвечает не "правильно ли действие выбрано" и не "безопасно ли его выполнить прямо сейчас", а "имеет ли система право продолжить выбранное действие в данном классе, масштабе, policy boundary, blast radius and risk envelope". |
| Mission justification | Без Authority сильный evidence, хороший Planner candidate, timer trigger или runtime readiness могут быть ошибочно приняты за permission to mutate production. Это ведет к silent blast-radius expansion, self-approval, unsafe autonomy and loss of operator/product control. |
| Mission success criteria | Каждое разрешенное действие имеет явный authority class, scope, budget, lifecycle state, policy boundary, approval/evidence basis and refusal behavior outside scope. |
| Mission failure criteria | V7 self-approves expansion, Runtime grants authority, packet approval is treated as durable product authority, technical possibility becomes production permission, or broad action passes as small scope. |
| Mission boundaries | Authority owns admission/approval boundary, action-class authority, delegated autonomy limits, blast-radius budget, authority lifecycle and refusal outside scope. It does not own observation, planning, identity lock, apply, verification, rollback, learning or closure. |
| Mission inputs | Planner candidate / selected moves, action class, policy context, certified outcomes, authority policy, operator approval, blast-radius evidence, risk, rollback/verification readiness, authority generation. |
| Mission outputs | Authority pass/stop, authority budget gate, authority lifecycle state, current allowed user budget, promotion/refusal result, approved authority envelope or explicit boundary reason. |
| Mission producers | Product Specification, Policy, Planner, OMP, Production Maturity, Engineering Reports, certified outcomes, operator/policy approval owners. |
| Mission consumers | Identity, Approved Plan Lock, Restore Barrier, Runtime, Execution, Verification, Rollback, CPS, OMP and Production Maturity. |
| Mission alternatives | Merge into Policy, Planner, Runtime, OMP, Identity, or use packet approval as authority. |
| Separate-domain justification | Policy defines rules, Planner selects, Runtime executes or stops, Identity locks object continuity, OMP certifies maturity; none of them should silently grant permission to expand action class or blast radius. |
| Mission-structure conclusion | Separate domain justified. |

## Mission Success Model

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Mission Success Criteria | Authority admits only explicitly scoped, bounded, certified or approved actions and refuses everything outside current scope. |
| Mission Failure Criteria | Any unapproved authority expansion, hidden self-approval, runtime-granted authority, stale packet treated as durable authority, or budget bypass. |
| Mission Boundaries | Authority is an admission boundary, not evidence, planning, identity, execution, verification, rollback or maturity scoring. |
| Mission Inputs | Action class, selected move scope, policy state, authority class, certified evidence, operator approval, blast radius, risk, authority generation. |
| Mission Outputs | `AUTHORITY_PASS` / authority stop, budget cap, lifecycle state, promotion/denial, boundary reason, authority envelope fields. |
| Mission Producers | Policy 004, Product Specification, OMP, Planner, certification reports, operator approval, Production Maturity. |
| Mission Consumers | Identity/lock owners, packet/restore barrier, Runtime, Execution, Verification, Rollback, CPS, OMP. |

## Source Coverage Ledger

## Trusted Knowledge Produced

Authority is the V7 admission boundary that decides whether an already selected intent may proceed inside approved class, scope, blast radius, risk and policy limits. It is not Planner, not Policy, not Runtime and not Production Maturity. Action-Class Authority is the durable product approval model; packet approval is only an ephemeral governed fallback. Current implementation already includes authority budget gates, lifecycle states, promotion rules, read-only enablement models and regression tests. Current production maturity is intentionally conservative: delegated autonomy is not approved and authority expansion is not granted by default. This is a maturity state, not an architecture failure.

## V7 Project Expert Output

Inside V7, Authority should be the admission boundary between a selected candidate and downstream identity/runtime execution. It answers whether a bounded action may proceed inside current class, scope, budget and policy authority. It must remain separate from Policy, Planner, Runtime and Production Maturity. V7 already maps this through OMP, Policy 004, Action-Class Authority, delegated policy preview, authority budget gate, approved packet/lock/restore barrier and Runtime stops.

## World Research Expert Output

Universal engineering laws require explicit, scoped, auditable and least-privilege authority. Detection is not authority, severity is not authority, readiness is not authority, and runtime permission is not proof of safety. Mature systems promote blast radius progressively, require operator or delegated policy approval for higher-risk boundaries, and separate authority expansion from routine execution inside approved scope.

## Implementation Expert Output

Implementation reality contains direct authority support: `AUTHORITY_CLASS_BUDGETS`, lifecycle states, `_authority_budget_gate`, `_authority_lifecycle_model`, `_authority_bridge_model`, `promote_authority`, delegated autonomy read models, action-class runtime enablement read models, rollback authority certification and tests. `_authority_budget_gate` is read-only/non-mutating and caps selected moves. `promote_authority` is mutation-capable but requires explicit confirmation, transition validity, truth check, evidence review, audit command and policy write rollback on audit failure.

## Knowledge Synthesis

All three realities agree: Authority is required as a separate boundary. The only apparent contradiction is maturity: POLICY_004 and Production Maturity show delegated authority is not fully approved, while implementation already has budget/promotion gates. This resolves cleanly: the Authority domain exists and is implemented for governed/budgeted operation; full delegated autonomy remains a maturity and policy lifecycle step.

| Treat packet approval as durable authority. | ADR rejects packet approval as primary product model. | Packet/Identity/Runtime artifact owners. |

## Certification Verdict

`CERTIFIED`

Domain 15 Authority is architecturally correct and should remain independent. It owns admission/refusal, action-class authority, blast-radius budget, lifecycle state, delegated-policy boundary and authority expansion guardrails. It must not plan, lock identity, execute, verify, rollback, learn or close outcomes. Current implementation is sufficient for domain certification through existing owners; broader delegated autonomy and authority evolution remain controlled maturity work, not an architecture gap.

# Domain 16 — Identity

Identity

## Executive Summary

Domain analyzed: 16 Identity.
Mission discovered: сохранить доказуемую непрерывность одного и того же production execution object от incident/evidence/decision/authority до Runtime, Verification, Rollback and Learning.
Ideal Domain Candidate: отдельный continuity / identity contract domain, который не выбирает, не разрешает и не исполняет действие, а фиксирует subject, incident, generation, selected_move_hash, packet/operation identity and fail-closed mismatch semantics.
What changed: архитектура V7 не изменилась; доверенное знание уточнено: Identity в V7 означает execution-object continuity, not user-auth identity.
Important gaps: blocking gaps не найдены.
Certification result: CERTIFIED.
Recommended next action: перейти к Domain 17 Runtime.

## Mission Discovery

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Fundamental engineering problem | V7 является распределенной автономной системой: разные владельцы видят evidence, incident, decision, authority, packet, restore barrier, apply, verification and rollback. Identity существует, чтобы доказать, что все эти владельцы говорят об одном и том же объекте, а не о похожем или заново пересобранном действии. |
| Mission justification | Без Identity система может корректно пройти Planner, Authority and Runtime gates, но применить, проверить, откатить или записать learning для другого user/source/target/incident/selected_move. Это ломает Reality First, Object Continuity and rollback safety. |
| Mission success criteria | Любой downstream owner может доказать `operation_id`, `packet_id`, `decision_id`, `authority_generation`, `restore_generation`, `selected_move_hash`, `user`, `source`, `target`, incident/source scope and semantic attempt continuity; mismatch fails closed or triggers explicit restart. |
| Mission failure criteria | Silent candidate switch, stale packet consumption, selected move replacement, source/target/user mismatch, incident-source drift, retry budget applied to wrong semantic attempt, verification of a different object, rollback under wrong identity. |
| Mission boundaries | Identity owns continuity contract, immutable committed execution identity, material identity comparison, mismatch detection and explicit restart requirement. It does not own planning, authority admission, restore-barrier creation, runtime apply, verification probing, rollback execution, learning or OMP promotion. |
| Mission inputs | Incident identity/source, selected move, planner generation, authority generation, packet/operation id, restore barrier generation/hash, source hashes, approved plan lock, current apply request identity, material state. |
| Mission outputs | Stable identity fields, selected move hash, immutable packet identity, approved identity binding status, material state change status, mismatch reasons, fail-closed identity blockers. |
| Mission producers | Incident, Planner, Authority, packet/approved-plan-lock owners, restore barrier, Runtime apply request, evidence source-hash owners. |
| Mission consumers | Approved Plan Lock, Restore Barrier, Runtime, Execution, Verification, Rollback / Closure, Learning, OMP, CPS, Engineering Reports. |
| Mission alternatives | Merge into Planner, Authority, Runtime, Verification, Rollback, OMP, or treat identity as report-only metadata. |
| Separate-domain justification | Planner selects, Authority admits, Runtime applies or stops, Verification checks outcome, Rollback compensates. None of those should silently own cross-owner continuity for every other owner. Identity must be a shared contract consumed by all, not a hidden side effect inside one stage. |
| Mission-structure conclusion | Separate domain justified. |

## Mission Success Model

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Mission Success Criteria | Same execution object preserved or explicit mismatch/identity restart recorded; no owner may silently reselect, replace users, replace targets, alter selected hash, or consume stale identity. |
| Mission Failure Criteria | Downstream execution, verification, rollback or learning acts on an object different from the approved/committed object. |
| Mission Boundaries | Continuity and mismatch detection only; no approval, mutation, probe, rollback, learning or maturity decision. |
| Mission Inputs | Identity-bearing fields from incident, planner, authority, packet, restore barrier and runtime request. |
| Mission Outputs | Immutable identity, selected hash, material-state comparison, binding verdict, mismatch reason. |
| Mission Producers | Incident / Planner / Authority / packet / restore barrier / runtime request owners. |
| Mission Consumers | Runtime, Execution, Verification, Rollback, Learning, OMP, reports and investigations. |

## Ideal Domain Candidate

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Candidate summary | Identity is the execution-object continuity domain for V7. It defines and enforces proof that every downstream owner consumes the same incident, selected move, user, source, target, generation, operation, packet, authority and restore identity, or stops explicitly on mismatch. |
| Mission solved | Prevent silent object replacement across distributed autonomous control-plane stages. |
| Mission success model | Same object preserved from incident/evidence to terminal outcome, or mismatch fail-closed with explicit reason. |
| Greenfield Verdict | YES |
| Greenfield Evidence | LAW R2, Runtime execution contract, L3 execution contract, R1/R2/R3/R4 identity/idempotency principles, packet/lease/approved-lock implementation and tests. |
| Greenfield Advantages | Strong rollback/verification correctness, no hidden recomputation, repeatable investigations, safe retries by semantic attempt. |
| Greenfield Disadvantages | Requires carrying extra identity fields and rejecting some executions that may appear safe but cannot prove continuity. |
| Greenfield Engineering Consequences | Keep Identity as separate domain; continue implementation through existing packet/lock/lease/barrier/runtime owners; no new Runtime/Planner/Authority. |
| Evidence foundation | System laws, Runtime Model, L3 capability, research, Function Graph, `operator_execution.py`, `v7-users-autoswitch`, tests. |
| Alternatives considered | Merge into Planner, Authority, Runtime, Verification, Rollback, OMP, or treat as report-only lineage. |
| Alternative ranking | See Alternative Ranking. |
| Reasons alternatives rejected | Each alternative collapses continuity into a stage that either does not see all downstream state or would approve/execute/verify its own changed object. |
| Trade-offs | Identity is a strict safety contract; it may stop executions when continuity is unproven, but that is required for autonomous correctness. |
| Architect decision required | ACCEPT. |

## Trusted Knowledge Produced

Identity в V7 является не пользовательской аутентификацией и не отдельным mutation owner. Это архитектурный контракт непрерывности execution object: один и тот же incident/source scope, selected move, user, source, target, generation, operation, packet, authority and restore identity must survive across Planner, Authority, Approved Plan Lock, Restore Barrier, Runtime, Verification, Rollback and Learning. The implementation reality supports this through immutable packet identity, approved packet binding, execution lease invalidation, approved plan lock validation, committed apply identity validation and tests. No new Identity owner is required; existing owners implement identity at their contract boundaries.

## V7 Project Expert Output

Inside V7, Identity should be the shared continuity contract that preserves the same production execution object across distributed owners. It should not become a selector, authority gate, runtime executor, verifier, rollback engine or learning owner. It must define what identity fields must remain stable and how the system stops when they do not.

## World Research Expert Output

Universal engineering principles require stable identity, idempotency keys, artifact lineage and exact rollback/verification subject identity across distributed automation. Retry budgets must be scoped by semantic attempt, and rollback/verification evidence must attach to the exact selected object or batch item.

## Implementation Expert Output

Implementation reality contains connected identity enforcement: packet/preview identity extraction, approved packet binding, immutable execution lease identity, material-state change gate, approved plan lock validation, committed apply identity validation and regression tests for matching/mismatching identity. No standalone mutating Identity owner exists; that absence is architecturally correct.

## Knowledge Synthesis

All three realities converge. V7 intent says object continuity is mandatory; research says distributed automation needs idempotency/lineage/exact rollback subject; implementation shows active fail-closed identity guards. The apparent absence of a standalone Identity runtime is not a gap, because Identity is a cross-owner contract enforced at existing owner boundaries.

# Domain 17 — Runtime

Runtime

## Executive Summary

Domain analyzed: 17 Runtime.
Mission discovered: быть тонкой live boundary, которая потребляет prepared/approved/locked artifacts, revalidates live gates and either executes through existing owners or STOP_SAFE.
Ideal Domain Candidate: thin execute-or-stop runtime domain, separate from Planner, Authority, Execution, Verification, Rollback, Learning and OMP.
What changed: архитектура V7 не изменилась; подтверждено, что implementation colocation in `v7-users-autoswitch.apply` is not architectural merger.
Important gaps: blocking gaps не найдены.
Certification result: CERTIFIED.
Recommended next action: перейти к Domain 18 Execution.

## Mission Discovery

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Fundamental engineering problem | V7 needs a final live safety boundary between approved intent and production-impacting execution. Prepared knowledge, Planner output, Authority approval and Identity lock can be valid earlier, but Runtime must decide at the last responsible moment whether the exact committed action may proceed or must stop safely. |
| Mission justification | Without Runtime, plans can become execution, stale packets can mutate production, authority can be bypassed, readiness can be confused with permission, and verification/rollback readiness can be discovered too late. Runtime protects users by making live apply eligibility bounded, explainable and fail-closed. |
| Mission success criteria | Runtime consumes only approved and identity-locked artifacts, revalidates authority/freshness/eligibility/safety/rollback/verification readiness, then either allows the exact action to Execution or emits explicit STOP_SAFE with reason. |
| Mission failure criteria | Runtime invents a decision, replans/reranks, expands authority, uses stale identity, treats apply as verified success, bypasses verification/rollback readiness, or silently retries duplicate work. |
| Mission boundaries | Runtime owns live execute-or-stop semantics, live gate enforcement, thin consumption of prepared knowledge, idempotency/loop guard consumption and handoff to Execution/Verification/Rollback/Closure. It does not own planning, authority expansion, actual success proof, rollback policy, long analytics, research, certification, learning or OMP promotion. |
| Mission inputs | Wake/incident/action context, Current Program State, decision snapshot, approved packet/lease, Identity, Authority, policy state, freshness, source/target eligibility, restore barrier, rollback/no-rollback readiness, verification readiness, blast radius and anti-flap/movement protection. |
| Mission outputs | Runtime allow/stop verdict, STOP_SAFE reason, runtime eligibility state, execution admission, duplicate/stale/loop blockers, terminal runtime result for CPS/OMP after downstream outcome closes. |
| Mission producers | Observation/Wake/Incident, Decision/Planner, Policy, Authority, Identity, packet/lease/restore barrier owners, readiness and freshness owners. |
| Mission consumers | Execution, Verification, Rollback / Closure, Learning, Current Program State, OMP, Engineering Reports, Production Maturity. |
| Mission alternatives | Merge into Planner, Authority, Execution, Verification, Rollback, OMP, or create a new daemon/runtime owner. |
| Separate-domain justification | Planner selects, Authority admits, Identity locks, Execution mutates, Verification proves, Rollback compensates and OMP certifies. Runtime is the final live gate that coordinates those contracts without becoming any of them. |
| Mission-structure conclusion | Separate domain justified. |

## Mission Success Model

| Field | Required answer |
| --- | --- |
| Mission Success Criteria | Runtime allows only exact committed approved identity inside current safety boundaries, or stops safely with precise reason and no mutation. |
| Mission Failure Criteria | Runtime becomes planner/authority/truth source or lets stale/unsafe/unverified actions proceed. |
| Mission Boundaries | Thin live execute-or-stop gate only; no broad computation or owner replacement. |
| Mission Inputs | Approved decision/packet/identity/authority/readiness/freshness/restore state. |
| Mission Outputs | Allow/STOP_SAFE, runtime eligibility, terminal runtime state for downstream owners. |
| Mission Producers | Upstream prepared and approval owners. |
| Mission Consumers | Execution, Verification, Rollback / Closure, Learning, CPS, OMP. |

## Trusted Knowledge Produced

Runtime in V7 is a thin live boundary, not a planner, authority engine, truth source, research system, dashboard, maturity owner or final success proof. It consumes prepared and approved artifacts, revalidates live gates and either admits exact Execution or records STOP_SAFE. Implementation reality has a real governed runtime path through existing owners: packet/lease/restore clearance, `v7-users-autoswitch --apply --verify`, L3 execution eligibility and terminal outcome handling. Physical colocation of apply/verify/rollback-adjacent code in `tools/v7-users-autoswitch` does not change the architecture because logical ownership and tests preserve runtime/execution/verification/rollback boundaries.

## V7 Project Expert Output

Inside V7, Runtime should be the thin live gate that consumes approved and identity-locked artifacts, validates live safety/authority/freshness/readiness, then either admits exact execution or STOP_SAFE. Runtime must not become Planner, Authority, Verification, Rollback, Learning, OMP, dashboard, research system or truth source.

## World Research Expert Output

World engineering principles support final live gates, idempotency, no hidden recomputation, readiness outside the irreversible critical path, explicit rollback/verification after mutation, and conservative stop behavior. Runtime must remain bounded and fail-closed.

## Implementation Expert Output

Implementation reality contains a connected governed runtime path: packet/lease/restore clearance in `operator_execution`, governed transaction orchestration, `run_autoswitch_apply`, `v7-users-autoswitch.apply`, L3 execution eligibility, `_run_switch`, verification/rollback handoff and terminal audit/learning closure. Tests cover explicit confirmation, budget denial, STOP_SAFE before apply, successful governed execution and downstream proof failure.

## Knowledge Synthesis

The strongest understanding is that Runtime is architecturally correct as a thin execute-or-stop boundary. The only apparent conflict is that code physically colocates runtime admission, mutation call, verification and rollback-adjacent handling. This is not an architecture defect because canonical documents and tests preserve logical ownership and because no new Runtime path is created.

## Certification Verdict

Existing implementation is sufficient for certification through governed autoswitch/runtime owners; no new Runtime owner is required.

# Domain 18 — Execution

## Executive Summary

Domain 18 Execution recovered from missing certification evidence and certified.

Mission discovered: define the exact production-impacting mutation/no-mutation boundary after Runtime admission and before Verification.

Ideal Domain Candidate: existing-owner execution boundary that applies only committed approved identity, discloses touched scope/result, and never treats no-op, preview or recommendation as success.

Current domain quality: `MATCHES_DISCOVERED_IDEAL`.

Important gaps: none blocking; physical code colocation with Runtime/Verification is non-blocking when contracts remain separated.

Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.

Recommended next action: proceed to Domain 19 Verification after persistence verification.

## Mission Discovery

Execution exists to solve the boundary problem between approved intent and changed production reality. It is the moment where V7 either mutates production through an approved object or explicitly does not mutate.

The domain is important because reports, previews, selected moves, authority objects, packets and runtime admission can all look like progress while no production reality changed.

Separate domain justified: yes. Runtime decides live execute-or-stop; Execution is the production-impacting apply/no-apply boundary; Verification proves whether the effect worked.

## Mission Success Model

Mission success criteria:

- actual mutation occurs only for the locked approved object;
- no-op/stop is disclosed as no mutation, not success;
- touched object scope is preserved for Verification, Rollback / Closure and Learning;
- downstream owners know exactly what was attempted and what changed.

Mission failure criteria:

- production changes without authority;
- wrong user/source/target is touched;
- no-op is hidden as success;
- verification is skipped;
- touched objects are not traceable.

## Ideal Domain Discovery

An ideal Execution domain should be the minimal bounded apply/no-apply layer. It should not choose, authorize, diagnose, verify or learn. It should expose exact touched scope and result.

## Ideal Domain Candidate

Execution is the production-impacting application boundary for committed approved identity. It applies exactly what Runtime admitted or safely does not apply, records what was touched, and hands result to Verification and Rollback / Closure.

## Current Domain Discovery

Current Domain 18 defines Execution as the actual production-impacting step that changes reality or intentionally stops before change. It excludes choosing action, authorization, verification outcome and learning maturity.

# Domain 19 — Verification

## Executive Summary

Domain 19 Verification recovered from missing certification evidence and certified.

Mission discovered: prove the actual outcome after production action against the same object and contract that was planned, authorized, locked and executed.

Ideal Domain Candidate: independent post-execution proof domain that distinguishes pass, fail and unknown, preserves object continuity, and feeds Rollback / Closure and Learning.

Current domain quality: `MATCHES_DISCOVERED_IDEAL`.

Important gaps: none blocking. Historical scoped-verification defects were implementation issues in existing owners and are now evidence that the domain boundary matters.

Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.

Recommended next action: proceed to Domain 20 Rollback / Closure after persistence verification.

## Mission Discovery

Verification exists because production mutation is not business success. A command can run, a route can change, or an apply can return zero while the user-visible and contract-level outcome is still wrong.

Separate domain justified: yes. Execution attempts mutation; Verification proves whether the intended outcome happened. Rollback / Closure consumes the result.

## Mission Success Model

Success criteria:

- verification checks the same user, source, target, service and action contract;
- pass, fail and unknown remain distinct;
- timeout, stale data and missing evidence do not silently become pass;
- result is sufficient for rollback, closure, learning and maturity.

Failure criteria:

- verifies wrong user/service/target;
- treats timeout as service fail without proof;
- treats unverified apply as success;
- changes success criteria after execution.

Verification is the post-execution proof domain. It consumes execution result and expected contract, runs bounded checks, classifies the outcome, and passes result to Rollback / Closure and Learning.

# Domain 20 — Rollback / Closure

## Executive Summary

Domain 20 Rollback / Closure recovered from missing certification evidence and certified.

Mission discovered: bring every production mutation, failed verification, unsafe unknown, partial outcome, or certified no-rollback case to a safe terminal state.

Ideal Domain Candidate: existing-owner terminal safety domain that compensates, contains, rolls back, safely closes, or proves canonical no-rollback/impossibility while preserving per-object outcome evidence.

Current domain quality: `MATCHES_DISCOVERED_IDEAL`.

Important gaps: none blocking.

Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.

Recommended next action: proceed to Domain 21 Learning after persistence verification.

## Mission Discovery

Rollback / Closure exists because production action cannot remain open-ended. A failed or unknown verification result must lead to compensation, containment, rollback, safe closure, or canonical impossibility.

Separate domain justified: yes. Verification proves outcome; Rollback / Closure decides and records the terminal operational safety state.

## Mission Success Model

Success criteria:

- every touched object reaches safe terminal state;
- rollback/no-rollback/containment/closure is explicit;
- partial success preserves per-object outcomes;
- closure evidence is consumable by Learning, Production Maturity and OMP.

Failure criteria:

- failed action remains open;
- residual risk is ignored;
- batch results collapse into ambiguous state;
- rollback/no-rollback success is assumed without evidence.

# Domain 21 — Learning

## Executive Summary

Domain 21 Learning recovered from missing certification evidence and certified.

Mission discovered: convert terminal outcomes into future evidence without learning from intent, synthetic evidence, packet existence, or unverified reports.

Ideal Domain Candidate: feedback/learning domain that consumes closed real outcomes, updates future decision/trust/maturity evidence, and preserves negative evidence.

Current domain quality: `MATCHES_DISCOVERED_IDEAL`.

Important gaps: none blocking.

Certification result: `CERTIFIED`.

Recommended next action: proceed to Domain 22 Production Maturity after persistence verification.

## Mission Discovery

Learning exists so V7 improves after real outcomes and stops repeating known-bad attempts. It solves the problem of turning closure evidence into future decision, trust, authority, maturity and automation evidence.

Separate domain justified: yes. Rollback / Closure closes the action; Learning updates future knowledge. Production Maturity consumes learning but must not produce it.

## Mission Success Model

Success criteria:

- terminal outcomes become future evidence;
- rollbacks, stops, unknowns and blocks remain evidence;
- synthetic/unverified evidence does not improve maturity;
- known bad semantic attempts are not repeated blindly.

Failure criteria:

- rollback is forgotten;
- failure becomes success learning;
- report existence becomes capability;
- synthetic outcomes improve authority or maturity.

| Source class | Inspected evidence | Coverage | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

# Domain 22 — Production Maturity

## Executive Summary

Domain 22 Production Maturity сертифицирован. Его mission — отделять инженерное понимание системы от реально доказанной производственной автономии. Идеальный кандидат совпадает с текущим доменом: Production Maturity является evidence-consuming maturity model, owned by OMP, and must not approve Runtime apply, expand Authority, move users, create evidence, or replace OMP. Архитектура не меняется. Восстановлен отсутствующий certification block для корпуса Phase 1.

## Mission Discovery

Production Maturity существует, чтобы V7 не объявляла автономию только потому, что архитектура, документы, тесты или модели выглядят завершенными. Фундаментальная проблема домена — измерить, насколько production capability реально earned через implementation, deploy, verification, certification, production outcomes, authority decisions and certified autonomy.

Если этот домен исчезнет, V7 начнет смешивать engineering completeness с production autonomy. Это создаст false autonomy: система может выглядеть готовой на бумаге, но не иметь достаточных реальных исходов для безопасной автоматизации.

Отдельный домен оправдан: OMP выбирает и продолжает программу, Current Program State показывает текущий volatile snapshot, Learning сохраняет outcomes, а Production Maturity именно принимает maturity decision на основе certified evidence.

## Mission Success Model

Mission Success Criteria:

- Production Maturity increases only from real evidence and certified outcomes.
- Engineering Maturity and Production Maturity remain separate.
- Maturity decisions are explicit: `ACCEPT`, `PARTIAL_ACCEPT`, `BLOCK`, `NO_CHANGE`, or `INVALID_EVIDENCE`.
- Current Program State and OMP can consume maturity state without treating it as Authority.

Mission Failure Criteria:

- Documentation completion is treated as production autonomy.
- Maturity is hand-edited as opinion.
- Production Maturity approves movement, expands Authority, creates evidence, or replaces OMP.

Mission Inputs: Engineering Reports, certification results, production outcomes, authority decisions, verified evidence, category status, backlog progress.

Mission Outputs: maturity decision, current maturity state, current target status, blockers, accepted or blocked maturity advancement.

Mission Producers: OMP, capability owners, certification owners, Engineering Reports, Verification, Learning, Authority decisions.

Mission Consumers: Current Program State, OMP, dashboard/read models, Product Observation, Engineering Report lifecycle.

## Source Coverage Ledger

| Source class | Coverage | Evidence used |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Knowledge Consolidation | COMPLETE | Domain 22 draft definition, responsibilities, relationships, success/failure criteria. |
| OMP / AOS / SYSTEM_MAP / CPS | COMPLETE | OMP owner relationship, CPS consumer role, SYSTEM_MAP production readiness mappings. |
| Current Program State / Production Maturity | COMPLETE | `V7_PRODUCTION_MATURITY_MODEL.md` purpose, separation, recalculation, behavior contract. |
| Canonical Reference / ADRs / Engineering Reports | COMPLETE | maturity-only-through-real-evidence rule and previous maturity update reports. |
| R1 / R2 / R3 / R4 | COMPLETE | operational maturity, progressive certification, evidence-backed promotion principles. |
| Function Graph Appendix / JSON / implementation | COMPLETE | read-only maturity projections and admin/operator visibility nodes; no mutation authority. |

## V7 Coverage Ledger

Production Maturity is canonical in `docs/reference/V7_PRODUCTION_MATURITY_MODEL.md`, with owner `OMP` and `Need New Owner: FALSE`. The model explicitly separates Engineering Maturity from Production Maturity and says Production Maturity does not redesign OMP, Runtime, Planner, Governance, execution, truth, policies, owners, authority, runtime apply, or user movement.

SYSTEM_MAP maps Production Maturity as connected to OMP, CPS and backlog, while still dependent on real production evidence. Current Program State consumes maturity state but cannot approve action.

## Research Coverage Ledger

R1/R2/R3/R4 support maturity as progressive, evidence-backed production readiness. Mature autonomous systems do not promote automation because a design exists; they use staged certification, real outcomes, operational excellence signals, rollback/verification evidence, and governance boundaries. Non-applicable research principles that would make maturity an execution authority were rejected.

## Implementation Coverage Ledger

Function Graph evidence shows maturity-like projections exist mainly as read-only/admin/operator visibility: `_knowledge_maturity_stage`, `build_knowledge_quality_read_model`, `build_acceleration_inventory`, operator pipeline maturity fields, and dashboard consumers. These implementation nodes expose maturity or readiness state but do not grant Runtime mutation authority. Tests such as `test_autonomous_knowledge_growth_program_exposes_cycle_maturity` cover maturity read-model behavior.

## Second-Pass Review

Contradiction search found no evidence that Production Maturity is allowed to move users, approve Runtime apply, expand Authority, or create production evidence. Implementation references to `PRODUCTION_AUTONOMY_READY` are readiness/read-model or authority-ladder signals, not proof that Production Maturity itself is an execution owner.

## Ideal Domain Discovery

The ideal domain is a maturity consumer and classifier. It must consume real evidence, distinguish engineering completion from production autonomy, produce explicit maturity decisions, expose maturity state to OMP/CPS, and reject synthetic, stale, duplicate, ownerless, or uncertified evidence.

## Ideal Domain Candidate

Domain 22 should be: "Production Maturity — the canonical evidence-consuming maturity model that records how far V7 is from certified production autonomy and whether specific evidence can advance maturity."

It must not become: Runtime, Authority, OMP, Learning, Engineering Report, dashboard, roadmap, or evidence producer.

## Current Domain Discovery

The current domain matches this candidate. It defines Production Maturity as a consumer of certified maturity impact after Engineering Reports, with required inputs, explicit decisions, outputs, and prohibited behaviors. The Knowledge Consolidation draft preserves the same boundary.

## Structural Comparison

| Dimension | Ideal | Current | Result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Mission | Measure earned production autonomy. | Same. | MATCH |
| Boundary | Consumer/classifier only. | Same. | MATCH |
| Authority | No execution approval. | Explicitly forbidden. | MATCH |
| Evidence | Real verified evidence only. | Same. | MATCH |
| Consumers | CPS, OMP, dashboard/read models. | Same. | MATCH |

## Architectural Equivalence

`EQUIVALENT`

The current domain and ideal candidate are architecturally equivalent. Existing implementation/read-model evidence supports the boundary; no new owner is required.

## Domain Existence Review

Separate domain remains justified. Merging into OMP would make the program navigator also the maturity classifier. Merging into CPS would turn volatile status into maturity truth. Merging into Learning would confuse outcome evidence with maturity decision. Merging into Authority would be unsafe because maturity is not permission.

## Trusted Knowledge

- Production Maturity is a consumer of certified evidence, not a producer of authority.
- Engineering Maturity and Production Maturity must never be merged.
- Production autonomy is earned through real implementation, deploy, verification, certification, production outcomes and authority decisions.
- `100%` Production Maturity means `PRODUCTION_AUTONOMY_CERTIFIED`, not documentation complete.
- Existing owners are sufficient; no new Production Maturity owner is required.

## Knowledge Delta

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Domain 22 certification evidence was missing from the corpus and has been recovered. | Corpus audit. | HIGH | Phase 1 certification corpus. | Engineering Reports |
| Production Maturity is certified as a consumer/classifier, not an execution or authority owner. | V7 docs + Function Graph. | HIGH | OMP, CPS, Authority, Runtime, Learning. | No Canonical Update Required |

## Architectural Decision Package

Current Domain: Domain 22 Production Maturity.

Ideal Domain Candidate: evidence-consuming maturity classifier for earned production autonomy.

Differences: no blocking difference.

Alternatives considered:

| Merge into OMP. | Rejected. | OMP runs the program; maturity classifies earned capability. |
| Merge into CPS. | Rejected. | CPS is volatile status; Production Maturity is maturity truth. |
| Merge into Authority. | Rejected. | Maturity is evidence state, not permission. |
| Create new owner. | Rejected. | Canonical model says owner OMP and Need New Owner FALSE. |

Recommendation: accept current Domain 22 as certified.

## Gap Discovery

No blocking architecture, ownership, runtime, authority, mutation or closure gap found. The recovered gap was certification corpus absence for this domain.

Domain 22 Production Maturity is architecturally correct, evidence-backed, and implementation-sufficient through existing owners and read-model surfaces.
